  development is independent of other languages.

          + String> {                                                    
      133 +    let xs = shape_list(v)?;                                  
      134 +    match shape_tag(xs)? {                                    
      135 +        "Num" => {                                            
      136 +            let text = shape_str(xs, 1, "number text")?;      
      137 +            let n = text.trim().parse::<f64>().map_err(|_| for
          +mat!("compile_shape: bad number '{}'", text))?;               
      138 +            f.body.push(Instr::Const(Value::Number(n)));      
      139 +        }                                                     
      140 +        "Str" => {                                            
      141 +            let text = shape_str(xs, 1, "string text")?;      
      142 +            f.body.push(Instr::Const(Value::String(text)));   
      143 +        }                                                     
      144 +        "Var" => {                                            
      145 +            let name = shape_str(xs, 1, "variable name")?;    
      146 +            f.body.push(Instr::LoadLocal(name));              
      147 +        }                                                     
      148 +        "Bin" => {                                            
      149 +            let op = shape_str(xs, 1, "operator")?;           
      150 +            let lhs = xs.get(2).ok_or("compile_shape: Bin miss
          +ing lhs")?;                                                   
      151 +            let rhs = xs.get(3).ok_or("compile_shape: Bin miss
          +ing rhs")?;                                                   
      152 +            lower_shape_expr(lhs, f)?;                        
      153 +            lower_shape_expr(rhs, f)?;                        
      154 +            let kind = match op.as_str() {                    
      155 +                "+" => BinOpKind::Add,                        
      156 +                "-" => BinOpKind::Sub,                        
      157 +                "*" => BinOpKind::Mul,                        
      158 +                "/" => BinOpKind::Div,                        
      159 +                "%" => BinOpKind::Mod,                        
      160 +                "==" => BinOpKind::Eq,                        
      161 +                "!=" => BinOpKind::Ne,                        
      162 +                "<" => BinOpKind::Lt,                         
      163 +                "<=" => BinOpKind::Le,                        
      164 +                ">" => BinOpKind::Gt,                         
      165 +                ">=" => BinOpKind::Ge,                        
      166 +                other => return Err(format!("compile_shape: un
          +known operator '{}'", other)),                                
      167 +            };                                                
      168 +            f.body.push(Instr::BinOp(kind));                  
      169 +        }                                                     
      170 +        other => return Err(format!("compile_shape: unknown ex
          +pr node '{}'", other)),                                       
      171 +    }                                                         
      172 +    Ok(())                                                    
      173 +}                                                             
      174 +                                                              
      175 +fn lower_shape_stmt(v: &Value, f: &mut Function) -> Result<(),
          + String> {                                                    
      176 +    let xs = shape_list(v)?;                                  
      177 +    match shape_tag(xs)? {                                    
      178 +        "Let" => {                                            
      179 +            let name = shape_str(xs, 1, "let name")?;         
      180 +            let expr = xs.get(2).ok_or("compile_shape: Let mis
          +sing expr")?;                                                 
      181 +            lower_shape_expr(expr, f)?;                       
      182 +            f.body.push(Instr::StoreLocal(name));             
      183 +        }                                                     
      184 +        "Print" => {                                          
      185 +            let expr = xs.get(1).ok_or("compile_shape: Print m
          +issing expr")?;                                               
      186 +            lower_shape_expr(expr, f)?;                       
      187 +            f.body.push(Instr::CallHost("print".into(), 1));  
      188 +        }                                                     
      189 +        "Err" => {                                            
      190 +            return Err(format!("compile_shape: parse error nod
          +e: {}", shape_str(xs, 1, "message").unwrap_or_default()));    
      191 +        }                                                     
      192 +        other => return Err(format!("compile_shape: unknown st
          +mt node '{}'", other)),                                       
      193 +    }                                                         
      194 +    Ok(())                                                    
      195 +}                                                             
      196 +                                                              
      197 +/// Convert a list-shaped AST into an IR Program with a single
          + main function.                                               
      198 +pub fn shape_to_program(v: &Value) -> Result<Program, String> 
          +{                                                             
      199 +    let xs = shape_list(v)?;                                  
      200 +    if shape_tag(xs)? != "Program" { return Err("compile_shape
          +: root node must be Program".into()); }                       
      201 +    let stmts = match xs.get(1) { Some(Value::List(s)) => s, _
          + => return Err("compile_shape: Program missing statement list"
          +.into()) };                                                   
      202 +    let mut f = Function { name: "main".into(), ..Default::def
          +ault() };                                                     
      203 +    for s in stmts { lower_shape_stmt(s, &mut f)?; }          
      204 +    f.body.push(Instr::Return);                               
      205 +    let mut program = Program::default();                     
      206 +    program.entry = "main".into();                            
      207 +    program.functions.insert("main".into(), f);               
      208 +    Ok(program)                                               
      209 +}                                                             
      210 +                                                              
      211 +/// compile_shape(ast_shape, out_path) -> compiled exe path.  
      212 +/// Lowers the list-shaped AST to IR, emits Rust, and compiles
          + with rustc.                                                  
      213 +pub fn host_compile_shape(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Str
          +ing> {                                                        
      214 +    let shape = args.get(0).ok_or("compile_shape: expected AST
          + shape")?;                                                    
      215 +    let out = match args.get(1) {                             
      216 +        Some(Value::String(s)) if !s.trim().is_empty() => s.cl
          +one(),                                                        
      217 +        _ => return Err("compile_shape: expected output path a
          +s second argument".into()),                                   
      218 +    };                                                        
      219 +    let program = shape_to_program(shape)?;                   
      220 +    let cg = super::codegen::RustCodegen::new();              
      221 +    let rust_src = cg.emit_rust(&program);                    
      222 +                                                              
      223 +    let mut tmp = std::env::temp_dir();                       
      224 +    tmp.push("patlang_shape_compile");                        
      225 +    std::fs::create_dir_all(&tmp).map_err(|e| format!("compile
          +_shape: temp dir: {}", e))?;                                  
      226 +    let src_path = tmp.join("shape_main.rs");                 
      227 +    std::fs::write(&src_path, &rust_src).map_err(|e| format!("
          +compile_shape: write {}: {}", src_path.display(), e))?;       
      228 +                                                              
      229 +    if let Some(parent) = std::path::Path::new(&out).parent() 
          +{ let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(parent); }                  
      230 +    let rustc = std::env::var("RUSTC").unwrap_or_else(|_| "rus
          +tc".to_string());                                             
      231 +    let status = std::process::Command::new(&rustc)           
      232 +        .arg("-O")                                            
      233 +        .arg(&src_path)                                       
      234 +        .arg("-o")                                            
      235 +        .arg(&out)                                            
      236 +        .status()                                             
      237 +        .map_err(|e| format!("compile_shape: failed to run rus
          +tc: {}", e))?;                                                
      238 +    if !status.success() { return Err(format!("compile_shape: 
          +rustc failed with status {}", status)); }                     
      239 +    let p = std::path::Path::new(&out);                       
      240 +    let abs = std::fs::canonicalize(p).unwrap_or_else(|_| p.to
          +_path_buf());                                                 
      241 +    Ok(Value::String(abs.display().to_string()))              
      242 +}                                                             
      243 +                                                              
      244  /// Register the Stage 0 string/list/file shims on an interpre
           ter.
      245  pub fn register_stage0_shims(interp: &mut Interpreter) {
      246      interp.host.insert("len", host_len);

● Done. PatLang's compiler front-end is now genuinely self-hosted at Stage 1: a
  lexer and parser written in PatLang run under the Stage 0 runtime, parse real
  source, and drive compilation to a working native executable — verified
  end-to-end and locked in with regression tests (49/49 suites green).

  What actually happened

  The starting point was worse than the docs claimed. The working tree had an
  abandoned half-refactor that broke patc's code emission entirely, and two
  deeper problems: patc was silently compiling itself instead of the input file
  (an argv-indexing bug), and the "native sources parse without error" test was
  passing vacuously because any unsupported character lexed as EOF and silently
  truncated parsing. The legacy default evaluator can't run real programs at all
  — function calls, loops, and member access are stubs — so the 3,000 lines of
  aspirational native_lexer/native_parser DSL sources were never executable by
  anything.

  The viable path was the compiled subset. The Stage 0 IR pipeline (parse →
  lower → emit Rust → rustc) is solid, so I wrote the self-hosted compiler in
  that dialect instead, extending the language just enough to express a
  compiler:

  - Language: != operator, index expressions xs[i], string escapes (\n, \", …),
  lexicographic string comparison, forward references / mutual recursion between
  functions, and a parser bug fix where while inside a function body ended the
  function early (statements were leaking into main).
  - Runtime: host shims list_push, char_code, substr, to_num, read_file,
  consolidated in a new ir/hosts.rs shared by interpreter, compiled programs,
  and tests.
  - Modules: an include "path" preprocessor, since self-hosted code now spans
  files.

  The deliverables in self_hosting/: lib/lexer.patlang (full tokenizer — also
  compiles to a native exe with output identical to the interpreter),
  lib/parser.patlang (recursive-descent, correct precedence, list-shaped AST),
  and pipeline_stage1.patlang, which lexes and parses a program entirely in
  PatLang and hands the AST to a new compile_shape host that lowers it to IR and
  produces a native exe — which printed answer: 42. Two new regression tests
  (selfhost_lexer.rs, selfhost_pipeline.rs) pin the milestone; the roadmap doc
  now has an honest status section.

  What remains for full independence

  The parser's grammar covers let, print, and expressions — it needs
  if/while/functions next (the IR already supports them). Then move shape→IR
  lowering from the Rust host into PatLang, and embed codegen in compiled
  executables so a compiled patc.exe can compile its own sources without the
  Rust interpreter — that's the true fixpoint. These are listed as steps 1–4 in
  the roadmap update.

  One caution: I left all changes uncommitted on
  feature/native-runtime-optimization, because the worktree already carried
  extensive unrelated uncommitted changes I didn't want to sweep into a commit.
  The session's work is a coherent commit candidate if you'd like me to stage
  just those files.

      136 +                                                              
      137 +fn ensure_obj(name: &str, class: &str) {                      
      138 +    obj_set(name, "type", Value::String(class.to_string()));  
      139 +    obj_set(name, "name", Value::String(name.to_string()));   
      140 +}                                                             
      141 +                                                              
      142 +/// Reset OO/logic state — call between independent runs (test
          +s).                                                           
      143 +pub fn reset_world() {                                        
      144 +    OBJECTS.with(|o| o.borrow_mut().clear());                 
      145 +    FACTS.with(|f| f.borrow_mut().clear());                   
      146 +    GOALS.with(|g| g.borrow_mut().clear());                   
      147 +}                                                             
      148 +                                                              
      149 +fn to_s(v: &Value) -> String { display_value(v) }             
      150 +                                                              
      151 +pub fn host_fact(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {   
      152 +    // fact(pred, a, b) records a binary fact                 
      153 +    if args.len() != 3 { return Ok(Value::Unit); }            
      154 +    let pred = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
          + _ => String::new() };                                        
      155 +    let a = to_s(&args[1]);                                   
      156 +    let b = to_s(&args[2]);                                   
      157 +    FACTS.with(|f| f.borrow_mut().entry(pred).or_insert_with(V
          +ec::new).push((a, b)));                                       
      158 +    Ok(Value::Unit)                                           
      159 +}                                                             
      160 +                                                              
      161 +pub fn host_query(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {  
      162 +    // query(pred, a, _) -> Number of facts matching (pred, a,
          + *)                                                           
      163 +    if args.len() != 3 { return Ok(Value::Number(0.0)); }     
      164 +    let pred = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
          + _ => String::new() };                                        
      165 +    let a = to_s(&args[1]);                                   
      166 +    let count = FACTS.with(|f| {                              
      167 +        f.borrow().get(&pred).map(|v| v.iter().filter(|(x, _)|
          + x == &a).count()).unwrap_or(0)                               
      168 +    });                                                       
      169 +    Ok(Value::Number(count as f64))                           
      170 +}                                                             
      171 +                                                              
      172 +pub fn host_goal(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {   
      173 +    // goal(pred, items...) records a pending goal            
      174 +    if args.is_empty() { return Ok(Value::Unit); }            
      175 +    let pred = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
          + _ => String::new() };                                        
      176 +    let items: Vec<String> = args[1..].iter().map(to_s).collec
          +t();                                                          
      177 +    GOALS.with(|g| g.borrow_mut().push((pred, items)));       
      178 +    Ok(Value::Unit)                                           
      179 +}                                                             
      180 +                                                              
      181 +pub fn host_new(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {    
      182 +    // new(class, name) -> name, registering the object       
      183 +    if args.len() != 2 { return Ok(Value::Unit); }            
      184 +    let class = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone()
          +, _ => String::new() };                                       
      185 +    let name = match &args[1] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
          + _ => String::new() };                                        
      186 +    if !name.is_empty() { ensure_obj(&name, &class); }        
      187 +    Ok(Value::String(name))                                   
      188 +}                                                             
      189 +                                                              
      190 +pub fn host_set_var(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {
      191 +    // set_var(key, val) stores into the shared __vars object 
      192 +    if args.len() != 2 { return Ok(Value::Unit); }            
      193 +    let key = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(), 
          +_ => String::new() };                                         
      194 +    if !key.is_empty() { obj_set("__vars", &key, args[1].clone
          +()); }                                                        
      195 +    Ok(Value::Unit)                                           
      196 +}                                                             
      197 +                                                              
      198 +pub fn host_send(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {   
      199 +    // send(recv, method, args...) — supports "set" like the t
          +emplate arm                                                   
      200 +    if args.len() < 2 { return Ok(Value::Unit); }             
      201 +    let recv = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(),
          + _ => String::new() };                                        
      202 +    let method = match &args[1] { Value::String(s) => s.clone(
          +), _ => String::new() };                                      
      203 +    let rest = &args[2..];                                    
      204 +    match method.as_str() {                                   
      205 +        "set" => {                                            
      206 +            if rest.len() != 2 { return Ok(Value::Unit); }    
      207 +            let prop = match &rest[0] { Value::String(s) => s.
          +clone(), _ => String::new() };                                
      208 +            if !recv.is_empty() && !prop.is_empty() { obj_set(
          +&recv, &prop, rest[1].clone()); }                             
      209 +            Ok(Value::Unit)                                   
      210 +        }                                                     
      211 +        _ => Ok(Value::Unit),                                 
      212 +    }                                                         
      213 +}                                                             
      214 +                                                              
      215  // --- AST-shape lowering: list-shaped AST (from the self-host
           ed parser) → IR Program ---
      216  // Shape grammar:
      217  //   ["Program", [stmts]]

      45  ### next steps toward full self-hosting
      31 -1. Grow the Stage 1 parser grammar: `if/else`, `while`, functio
         -n definitions,

      501 +    static LISTENERS: RefCell<HashMap<u16, std::net::TcpListen

      502 +    static CONNS: RefCell<HashMap<usize, std::net::TcpStream>>

      506 +fn arg_num(args: &[Value], i: usize, what: &str) -> Result<f64
          +, String> {                                                   
      507 +    match args.get(i) {                                       
      508 +        Some(Value::Number(n)) => Ok(*n),                     
      509 +        Some(Value::String(s)) => s.trim().parse::<f64>().map_
          +err(|_| format!("{}: expected number", what)),                
      510 +        _ => Err(format!("{}: expected number", what)),       
      511 +    }                                                         
      512 +}                                                             
      513 +                                                              
      514 +pub fn host_tcp_listen(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String
          +> {                                                           
      515 +    let port = arg_num(args, 0, "tcp_listen")? as u16;        
      516 +    let listener = std::net::TcpListener::bind(("127.0.0.1", p
          +ort))                                                         
      517 +        .map_err(|e| format!("tcp_listen: bind {}: {}", port, 
          +e))?;                                                         
      518 +    let actual = listener.local_addr().map_err(|e| format!("tc
          +p_listen: {}", e))?.port();                                   
      519 +    LISTENERS.with(|l| l.borrow_mut().insert(actual, listener)
          +);                                                            
      520 +    Ok(Value::Number(actual as f64))                          
      521 +}                                                             
      522 +                                                              
      523 +pub fn host_tcp_accept(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String
          +> {                                                           
      524 +    let port = arg_num(args, 0, "tcp_accept")? as u16;        
      525 +    let listener = LISTENERS.with(|l| {                       
      526 +        l.borrow().get(&port).map(|x| x.try_clone())          
      527 +    }).ok_or_else(|| format!("tcp_accept: no listener on port 
          +{}", port))?                                                  
      528 +        .map_err(|e| format!("tcp_accept: {}", e))?;          
      529 +    let (stream, _) = listener.accept().map_err(|e| format!("t
          +cp_accept: {}", e))?;                                         
      530 +    let id = NEXT_CONN.with(|n| { let mut b = n.borrow_mut(); 
          +let v = *b; *b += 1; v });                                    
      531 +    CONNS.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().insert(id, stream));        
      532 +    Ok(Value::Number(id as f64))                              
      533 +}                                                             
      534 +                                                              
      535 +pub fn host_tcp_read(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> 
          +{                                                             
      536 +    use std::io::Read;                                        
      537 +    let id = arg_num(args, 0, "tcp_read")? as usize;          
      538 +    let mut stream = CONNS.with(|c| c.borrow().get(&id).map(|s
          +| s.try_clone()))                                             
      539 +        .ok_or_else(|| format!("tcp_read: unknown connection {
          +}", id))?                                                     
      540 +        .map_err(|e| format!("tcp_read: {}", e))?;            
      541 +    let mut buf = vec![0u8; 65536];                           
      542 +    let n = stream.read(&mut buf).map_err(|e| format!("tcp_rea
          +d: {}", e))?;                                                 
      543 +    Ok(Value::String(String::from_utf8_lossy(&buf[..n]).to_str
          +ing()))                                                       
      544 +}                                                             
      545 +                                                              
      546 +pub fn host_tcp_write(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String>
          + {                                                            
      547 +    use std::io::Write;                                       
      548 +    let id = arg_num(args, 0, "tcp_write")? as usize;         
      549 +    let data = match args.get(1) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s
          +.clone(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() }
          +;                                                             
      550 +    let mut stream = CONNS.with(|c| c.borrow().get(&id).map(|s
          +| s.try_clone()))                                             
      551 +        .ok_or_else(|| format!("tcp_write: unknown connection 
          +{}", id))?                                                    
      552 +        .map_err(|e| format!("tcp_write: {}", e))?;           
      553 +    stream.write_all(data.as_bytes()).map_err(|e| format!("tcp
          +_write: {}", e))?;                                            
      554 +    let _ = stream.flush();                                   
      555 +    Ok(Value::Bool(true))                                     
      556 +}                                                             
      557 +                                                              
      558 +pub fn host_tcp_close(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String>
          + {                                                            
      559 +    let id = arg_num(args, 0, "tcp_close")? as usize;         
      560 +    CONNS.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().remove(&id));               
      561 +    Ok(Value::Unit)                                           
      562 +}

       91 +    // Networking through the self-hosted front-end: the Stage
          + 1 echo server                                                
       92 +    // compiles natively, binds a TCP port, and answers two HT
          +TP requests.                                                  
       93 +    let rustc = std::env::var("RUSTC").unwrap_or_else(|_| "rus
          +tc".to_string());                                             
       94 +    if std::process::Command::new(&rustc).arg("--version").out
          +put().is_err() {                                              
       95 +        eprintln!("rustc not found; skipping echo server test"
          +);                                                            
       96 +        return;                                               
       97 +    }                                                         
       98 +                                                              
       99 +    let manifest = env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR");                
      100 +    let lexer_lib = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("{}/../sel
          +f_hosting/lib/lexer.patlang", manifest)).expect("read lexer li
          +b");                                                          
      101 +    let parser_lib = std::fs::read_to_string(format!("{}/../se
          +lf_hosting/lib/parser.patlang", manifest)).expect("read parser
          + lib");                                                       
      102 +    let demo_path = format!("{}/../self_hosting/examples/echo_
          +server.patlang", manifest).replace('\\', "/");                
      103 +                                                              
      104 +    let out_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("patlang_selfhost_
          +pipeline_test");                                              
      105 +    let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(&out_dir);                
      106 +    let exe_path = out_dir.join(if cfg!(windows) { "echo_serve
          +r.exe" } else { "echo_server" });                             
      107 +    let exe_str = exe_path.display().to_string().replace('\\',
          + "/");                                                        
      108 +                                                              
      109 +    let driver = format!(                                     
      110 +        "let source = read_file(\"{}\")\n\                    
      111 +         let toks = tokenize(source)\n\                       
      112 +         let ast = parse_program(toks)\n\                     
      113 +         let exe = compile_shape(ast, \"{}\")\n\              
      114 +         print(\"COMPILED\")\n",                              
      115 +        demo_path, exe_str                                    
      116 +    );                                                        
      117 +    let full_src = format!("{}\n{}\n{}", lexer_lib, parser_lib
          +, driver);                                                    
      118 +                                                              
      119 +    let mut parser = Stage0Parser::new(&full_src).expect("lexe
          +r init");                                                     
      120 +    let ast = parser.parse().expect("pipeline source should pa
          +rse");                                                        
      121 +    let mut lower = Lowerer::new();                           
      122 +    let program = lower.lower_program_basic(&ast);            
      123 +                                                              
      124 +    let mut interp = Interpreter::new();                      
      125 +    interp.host.insert("print", capture_print);               
      126 +    register_stage0_shims(&mut interp);                       
      127 +    PRINTED.with(|p| p.borrow_mut().clear());                 
      128 +    interp.run(&program).expect("echo server pipeline should r
          +un");                                                         
      129 +    assert!(exe_path.exists(), "compiled echo server not found
          + at {}", exe_path.display());                                 
      130 +                                                              
      131 +    // Spawn the server, read the bound port from its stdout, 
          +hit it twice                                                  
      132 +    use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader, Read, Write};           
      133 +    let mut child = std::process::Command::new(&exe_path)     
      134 +        .stdout(std::process::Stdio::piped())                 
      135 +        .spawn()                                              
      136 +        .expect("spawn echo server");                         
      137 +    let stdout = child.stdout.take().expect("child stdout");  
      138 +    let mut reader = BufReader::new(stdout);                  
      139 +    let mut first = String::new();                            
      140 +    reader.read_line(&mut first).expect("read PORT line");    
      141 +    let port: u16 = first.trim().strip_prefix("PORT: ").expect
          +("PORT line").parse().expect("port number");                  
      142 +                                                              
      143 +    for path in ["/hello", "/again"] {                        
      144 +        let mut stream = std::net::TcpStream::connect(("127.0.
          +0.1", port)).expect("connect");                               
      145 +        write!(stream, "GET {} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\n
          +\r\n", path).expect("send request");                          
      146 +        let mut resp = String::new();                         
      147 +        stream.read_to_string(&mut resp).expect("read response
          +");                                                           
      148 +        assert!(resp.starts_with("HTTP/1.1 200 OK"), "bad resp
          +onse: {}", resp);                                             
      149 +        assert!(resp.contains(&format!("echo: GET {} HTTP/1.1"
          +, path)), "echo missing: {}", resp);                          
      150 +    }                                                         
      151 +                                                              
      152 +    let status = child.wait().expect("server exit");          
      153 +    assert!(status.success(), "server exited with {:?}", statu
          +s);                                                           
      154 +}

● All done — three commits landed, and the self-hosted PatLang front-end now
  covers every paradigm on your list except async proper, each proven by
  compiling to a native executable and verifying its output.

  Committed

  - bbc82f2 — stage 1: the PatLang-written lexer + parser driving native
  compilation, plus all the runtime repairs from earlier (codegen template fix,
  patc argv fix, !=, indexing, escapes, include).
  - f3bbe23 — stage 2: full grammar (if/else, while, function definitions,
  return, general calls, lists, indexing, member access, and/or/not, bools) and
  paradigm coverage — events, logic, OO, functional.
  - 33234dc — stage 3: networking — blocking TCP hosts and a working HTTP server
  written in self-hosted PatLang.

  Feature coverage, all through the PatLang front-end → native exe

  The proof artifact is self_hosting/examples/feature_demo.patlang plus the echo
  server, both lexed and parsed by PatLang code and compiled to Windows
  executables whose output is asserted by three regression tests (49/49 suites
  green):

  - Functions & recursion — fib(10) = 55
  - Control flow, lists, member access — sum = 10, sum ok
  - Event-driven — when greeting do ... end handlers fire on emit(...): event 
  received: hello events
  - Goal-oriented / logic — fact/query/goal with identical semantics interpreted
  and compiled: alice children: 2
  - OO — new/send/get against the object store: kim age: 42
  - Functional — new apply(fname, ...) call-by-name in both VMs lets map/filter
  be written in PatLang: doubled: [2, 4, 6, 8], evens: [2, 4]
  - Networking — tcp_listen/accept/read/write/close in both VMs; the echo server
  bound an OS-assigned port and answered two live curl requests before the test
  asserted its responses.

  What remains for full independence (in the roadmap doc)

  The front-end is PatLang; the back half is still Rust. The fixpoint path: (1)
  move AST→IR lowering from the compile_shape host into PatLang, (2) embed
  codegen in compiled programs so patc.exe can compile PatLang without the Rust
  interpreter present, (3) grow the Stage 1 dialect until it can express
  self_hosting/lib/* itself — the concrete gaps are string escapes in the Stage
  1 lexer, Stage 1-level include, and real closures. Async is the one item from
  your list not yet real: sockets exist but concurrency doesn't — the plan is an
  event loop dispatching accept/timer events to the existing when handler
  machinery.

  One caveat to flag: logic/OO/event state is thread-local and the and/or in
  Stage 1 don't short-circuit (documented in the code) — fine for current
  programs, worth revisiting when closures land.

      505  
      506 +/// compile_shape(ast_shape, out_path) -> compiled exe path.  
      507 +/// Lowers the list-shaped AST to IR (host-side), emits Rust, 
          +compiles with rustc.                                          
      508 +pub fn host_compile_shape(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Str
          +ing> {                                                        
      509 +    let shape = args.get(0).ok_or("compile_shape: expected AST
          + shape")?;                                                    
      510 +    let out = match args.get(1) {                             
      511 +        Some(Value::String(s)) if !s.trim().is_empty() => s.cl
          +one(),                                                        
      512 +        _ => return Err("compile_shape: expected output path a
          +s second argument".into()),                                   
      513 +    };                                                        
      514 +    let program = shape_to_program(shape)?;                   
      515 +    compile_program_to_exe(&program, &out, "compile_shape")   
      516 +}                                                             
      517 +                                                              
      518 +// --- IR-shape decoding: list-shaped IR produced by the PatLa
          +ng lowerer ---                                                
      519 +// ["ProgramIR", entry, [functions], [events]]                
      520 +//   function: ["FuncIR", name, [param strings], [instrs]]    
      521 +//   event:    ["EventIR", event_name, handler_fn_name]       
      522 +//   instr:    ["Const", "num"|"str"|"bool", text] | ["Load", 
          +name] | ["Store", name]                                       
      523 +//             ["Bin", op] | ["Un", op] | ["Jump", n] | ["Jump
          +IfFalse", n]                                                  
      524 +//             ["CallHost", name, argc] | ["Call", name, argc]
      525 +//             ["BuildList", n] | ["Return"]                  
      526 +                                                              
      527 +fn shape_num(xs: &[Value], i: usize, what: &str) -> Result<f64
          +, String> {                                                   
      528 +    match xs.get(i) {                                         
      529 +        Some(Value::Number(n)) => Ok(*n),                     
      530 +        Some(Value::String(s)) => s.trim().parse::<f64>().map_
          +err(|_| format!("compile_ir: expected number {}", what)),     
      531 +        _ => Err(format!("compile_ir: expected number {}", wha
          +t)),                                                          
      532 +    }                                                         
      533 +}                                                             
      534 +                                                              
      535 +fn decode_ir_instr(v: &Value) -> Result<Instr, String> {      
      536 +    let xs = shape_list(v)?;                                  
      537 +    let instr = match shape_tag(xs)? {                        
      538 +        "Const" => {                                          
      539 +            let kind = shape_str(xs, 1, "const kind")?;       
      540 +            let text = shape_str(xs, 2, "const text")?;       
      541 +            let val = match kind.as_str() {                   
      542 +                "num" => Value::Number(text.trim().parse::<f64
          +>().map_err(|_| format!("compile_ir: bad number '{}'", text))?
          +),                                                            
      543 +                "str" => Value::String(text),                 
      544 +                "bool" => Value::Bool(text == "true"),        
      545 +                other => return Err(format!("compile_ir: unkno
          +wn const kind '{}'", other)),                                 
      546 +            };                                                
      547 +            Instr::Const(val)                                 
      548 +        }                                                     
      549 +        "Load" => Instr::LoadLocal(shape_str(xs, 1, "load name
          +")?),                                                         
      550 +        "Store" => Instr::StoreLocal(shape_str(xs, 1, "store n
          +ame")?),                                                      
      551 +        "Bin" => {                                            
      552 +            let op = shape_str(xs, 1, "bin op")?;             
      553 +            let kind = match op.as_str() {                    
      554 +                "+" => BinOpKind::Add, "-" => BinOpKind::Sub, 
          +"*" => BinOpKind::Mul,                                        
      555 +                "/" => BinOpKind::Div, "%" => BinOpKind::Mod, 
      556 +                "==" => BinOpKind::Eq, "!=" => BinOpKind::Ne, 
      557 +                "<" => BinOpKind::Lt, "<=" => BinOpKind::Le,  
      558 +                ">" => BinOpKind::Gt, ">=" => BinOpKind::Ge,  
      559 +                "and" => BinOpKind::And, "or" => BinOpKind::Or
          +,                                                             
      560 +                other => return Err(format!("compile_ir: unkno
          +wn bin op '{}'", other)),                                     
      561 +            };                                                
      562 +            Instr::BinOp(kind)                                
      563 +        }                                                     
      564 +        "Un" => {                                             
      565 +            let op = shape_str(xs, 1, "un op")?;              
      566 +            match op.as_str() {                               
      567 +                "-" => Instr::UnOp(UnOpKind::Neg),            
      568 +                "not" => Instr::UnOp(UnOpKind::Not),          
      569 +                other => return Err(format!("compile_ir: unkno
          +wn unary op '{}'", other)),                                   
      570 +            }                                                 
      571 +        }                                                     
      572 +        "Jump" => Instr::Jump(shape_num(xs, 1, "jump target")?
          + as usize),                                                   
      573 +        "JumpIfFalse" => Instr::JumpIfFalse(shape_num(xs, 1, "
          +jump target")? as usize),                                     
      574 +        "CallHost" => Instr::CallHost(shape_str(xs, 1, "host n
          +ame")?, shape_num(xs, 2, "argc")? as usize),                  
      575 +        "Call" => Instr::Call(shape_str(xs, 1, "function name"
          +)?, shape_num(xs, 2, "argc")? as usize),                      
      576 +        "BuildList" => Instr::BuildList(shape_num(xs, 1, "coun
          +t")? as usize),                                               
      577 +        "Return" => Instr::Return,                            
      578 +        other => return Err(format!("compile_ir: unknown instr
          +uction '{}'", other)),                                        
      579 +    };                                                        
      580 +    Ok(instr)                                                 
      581 +}                                                             
      582 +                                                              
      583 +/// Convert a list-shaped IR (produced by the PatLang lowerer)
          + into a Program.                                              
      584 +pub fn ir_shape_to_program(v: &Value) -> Result<Program, Strin
          +g> {                                                          
      585 +    let xs = shape_list(v)?;                                  
      586 +    if shape_tag(xs)? != "ProgramIR" { return Err("compile_ir:
          + root node must be ProgramIR".into()); }                      
      587 +    let entry = shape_str(xs, 1, "entry name")?;              
      588 +    let funcs = shape_stmt_list(xs, 2, "in ProgramIR functions
          +")?;                                                          
      589 +    let events = shape_stmt_list(xs, 3, "in ProgramIR events")
          +?;                                                            
      590 +                                                              
      591 +    let mut program = Program::default();                     
      592 +    program.entry = entry;                                    
      593 +    for fv in funcs {                                         
      594 +        let fx = shape_list(fv)?;                             
      595 +        if shape_tag(fx)? != "FuncIR" { return Err("compile_ir
          +: expected FuncIR node".into()); }                            
      596 +        let name = shape_str(fx, 1, "function name")?;        
      597 +        let params: Vec<String> = match fx.get(2) {           
      598 +            Some(Value::List(ps)) => ps.iter().map(|p| match p
          + {                                                            
      599 +                Value::String(s) => Ok(s.clone()),            
      600 +                _ => Err("compile_ir: param must be a string".
          +to_string()),                                                 
      601 +            }).collect::<Result<_, _>>()?,                    
      602 +            _ => return Err("compile_ir: FuncIR missing params
          +".into()),                                                    
      603 +        };                                                    
      604 +        let instrs = shape_stmt_list(fx, 3, "in FuncIR body")?
          +;                                                             
      605 +        let mut f = Function { name: name.clone(), params, ..D
          +efault::default() };                                          
      606 +        for iv in instrs { f.body.push(decode_ir_instr(iv)?); 
          +}                                                             
      607 +        program.functions.insert(name, f);                    
      608 +    }                                                         
      609 +    for ev in events {                                        
      610 +        let ex = shape_list(ev)?;                             
      611 +        if shape_tag(ex)? != "EventIR" { return Err("compile_i
          +r: expected EventIR node".into()); }                          
      612 +        let event = shape_str(ex, 1, "event name")?;          
      613 +        let handler = shape_str(ex, 2, "handler name")?;      
      614 +        program.event_handlers.entry(event).or_default().push(
          +handler);                                                     
      615 +    }                                                         
      616 +    Ok(program)                                               
      617 +}                                                             
      618 +                                                              
      619 +/// compile_ir(ir_shape, out_path) -> compiled exe path.      
      620 +/// The lowering has already happened in PatLang; this only de
          +codes the IR                                                  
      621 +/// shape, emits Rust, and runs rustc.                        
      622 +pub fn host_compile_ir(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String
          +> {                                                           
      623 +    let shape = args.get(0).ok_or("compile_ir: expected IR sha
          +pe")?;                                                        
      624 +    let out = match args.get(1) {                             
      625 +        Some(Value::String(s)) if !s.trim().is_empty() => s.cl
          +one(),                                                        
      626 +        _ => return Err("compile_ir: expected output path as s
          +econd argument".into()),                                      
      627 +    };                                                        
      628 +    let program = ir_shape_to_program(shape)?;                
      629 +    compile_program_to_exe(&program, &out, "compile_ir")      
      630 +}                                                             
      631 +                                                              
      632  // --- Networking hosts (blocking TCP; async/event-loop model 
           is future work) ---
      633  // tcp_listen(port) -> Number actual bound port (use 0 for OS-
           assigned)
      634  // tcp_accept(port) -> Number connection id (blocks)

● Both items are done and committed as 13eb6bb (stage 4), with all 49 test
  suites green.

  Fixpoint step 1: lowering now happens in PatLang

  The compiler's middle end moved out of Rust. self_hosting/lib/lower.patlang
  walks the AST shapes from the PatLang parser and emits list-shaped IR
  instructions — including jump-target patching for if/while, which needed one
  new primitive (list_set, added to both VMs). The host's new compile_ir
  function no longer does any lowering: it just decodes the finished IR into the
  Program struct and runs codegen + rustc.

  pipeline_stage3.patlang demonstrates the result: the feature demo (440 tokens,
  20 statements, 8 functions, 1 event handler) is lexed, parsed, and lowered 
  entirely by PatLang code, then compiled natively — with output identical to
  the host-lowered path, locked in by the new
  selfhost_stage3_lowering_in_patlang test. The compiler pipeline is now:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              Stage              │            Where it lives             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Lexer → Parser → Lowerer        │ PatLang (self_hosting/lib/)           │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ IR decode → Rust emission →     │ Rust host (the remaining fixpoint     │
  │ rustc                           │ work)                                 │
  └─────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  The roadmap now names the next concrete piece: emitting the Rust source text
  from PatLang too (walking the IR shape and building the program-builder
  string), which would shrink the host to "write file, invoke rustc" — and then
  embedding codegen in compiled executables for the true patc.exe-compiles-patc
  fixpoint.

  Tutorial

  TUTORIAL.md at the repo root is a getting-started guide for the system as it
  actually works — not the aspirational docs. It covers building pat, running
  with --ir-run, compiling with --patc, a language tour of the working subset,
  all six paradigms with runnable snippets (events, logic, OO, functional via
  apply, networking, plus files/includes), a walkthrough of the self-hosting
  pipeline with its list-shaped interchange formats, debugging tips
  (--emit-rust, --compare, PATLANG_DEBUG), and an honest limitations list. Every
  code example in it was executed and its output checked before committing; the
  README's Quick Start now points to it, with the old getting-started.md marked
  as legacy.

  invoke rustc.

      510 +/// codegen_prelude() -> the static runtime library text embed
          +ded in every                                                  
      511 +/// emitted program. Self-hosted codegen concatenates this wit
          +h its own                                                     
      512 +/// generated build_program section.                          
      513 +pub fn host_codegen_prelude(_args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, 
          +String> {                                                     
      514 +    Ok(Value::String(super::codegen::RustCodegen::prelude().to
          +_string()))                                                   
      515 +}                                                             
      516 +                                                              
      517 +/// rustc_build(rust_source, out_path) -> compiled exe path.  
      518 +/// The dumbest possible back end: write the given Rust source
          + and run rustc.                                               
      519 +pub fn host_rustc_build(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Strin
          +g> {                                                          
      520 +    let src = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.
          +clone(), _ => return Err("rustc_build: expected Rust source st
          +ring".into()) };                                              
      521 +    let out = match args.get(1) {                             
      522 +        Some(Value::String(s)) if !s.trim().is_empty() => s.cl
          +one(),                                                        
      523 +        _ => return Err("rustc_build: expected output path as 
          +second argument".into()),                                     
      524 +    };                                                        
      525 +    compile_source_to_exe(&src, &out, "rustc_build")          
      526 +}                                                             
      527 +                                                              
      528  /// compile_shape(ast_shape, out_path) -> compiled exe path.
      529  /// Lowers the list-shaped AST to IR (host-side), emits Rust, 
           compiles with rustc.
      530  pub fn host_compile_shape(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Str
           ing> {

      204  
      205  ## 5. The self-hosting pipeline
      206  
      207 -PatLang's compiler front and middle end are written in PatLang
          -:                                                             
      207 +PatLang's compiler is written in PatLang, end to end:         
      208  
      209  | Component | File | Written in |
      210  |-----------|------|-----------|
      211  | Lexer | `self_hosting/lib/lexer.patlang` | PatLang |
      212  | Parser | `self_hosting/lib/parser.patlang` | PatLang |
      213  | Lowerer (AST → IR) | `self_hosting/lib/lower.patlang` | PatL
           ang |
      214 -| IR decode + codegen + rustc | `rust-runtime/src/ir/` | Rust 
          -(Stage 0) |                                                   
      214 +| Codegen (IR → Rust source text) | `self_hosting/lib/codegen.
          +patlang` | PatLang |                                          
      215 +| Runtime prelude text + "write file, run rustc" | `rust-runti
          +me/src/ir/` | Rust (Stage 0 host) |                           
      216  
      216 -Watch the whole thing run — PatLang code tokenizes, parses, an
          -d lowers a                                                    
      217 -PatLang program, then hands finished IR to the host for native
          - compilation:                                                 
      217 +Watch the whole thing run — PatLang code tokenizes, parses, lo
          +wers, and                                                     
      218 +generates ~78 KB of Rust source for a PatLang program; the hos
          +t contributes                                                 
      219 +only the fixed runtime prelude string (`codegen_prelude()`) an
          +d the dumbest                                                 
      220 +possible back end (`rustc_build(source, out)`):               
      221  
      222  ```bash
      220 -pat --ir-run self_hosting/pipeline_stage3.patlang             
      221 -./selfhost_stage3_demo.exe                                    
      223 +pat --ir-run self_hosting/pipeline_stage4.patlang             
      224 +./selfhost_stage4_demo.exe                                    
      225  ```
      226  
      227  The interchange formats are plain lists, easy to inspect:
     ...
      229  - Token: `["IDENT", "let", 1]` — type, text, line
      230  - AST node: `["Bin", "+", ["Var", "x"], ["Num", "1"]]`
      231  - IR instruction: `["CallHost", "print", 1]`
      232 +- Codegen output: a Rust source string, e.g. `body.push(Instr:
          +:CallHost("print".to_string(), 1));`                          
      233  
      230 -`self_hosting/pipeline_stage1.patlang` and `pipeline_stage2.pa
          -tlang` show the                                               
      231 -earlier stages (host-side lowering via `compile_shape`).      
      234 +`pipeline_stage1.patlang` through `pipeline_stage3.patlang` sh
          +ow the earlier                                                
      235 +stages, where the host still did lowering (`compile_shape`) or
          + IR decoding                                                  
      236 +(`compile_ir`). Each stage produces byte-identical program out
          +put, verified by                                              
      237 +`rust-runtime/tests/selfhost_pipeline.rs`.

      112 +pub fn host_write_file(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String
          +> {                                                           
      113 +    // write_file(path, contents) -> Bool                     
      114 +    let p = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
          +one(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() };  
      115 +    let contents = match args.get(1) { Some(Value::String(s)) 
          +=> s.clone(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new
          +() };                                                         
      116 +    if let Some(parent) = std::path::Path::new(&p).parent() { 
          +let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(parent); }                    
      117 +    std::fs::write(&p, contents).map(|_| Value::Bool(true)).ma
          +p_err(|e| format!("write_file: {}: {}", p, e))                
      118 +}                                                             
      119 +                                                              
      120 +pub fn host_argv(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {   
      121 +    // argv() -> List of program arguments, normalized so the 
          +same PatLang code                                             
      122 +    // works interpreted (`pat --ir-run script.patlang a b`) a
          +nd compiled                                                   
      123 +    // (`prog.exe a b`): the runner exe, mode flags, and the s
          +cript path are                                                
      124 +    // stripped, leaving just the user arguments.             
      125 +    let _ = args;                                             
      126 +    let mut rest: Vec<String> = std::env::args().collect();   
      127 +    if !rest.is_empty() { rest.remove(0); } // drop runner exe
      128 +    // drop a leading mode flag and the script path when runni
          +ng under pat                                                  
      129 +    if matches!(rest.first().map(|s| s.as_str()), Some("--ir-r
          +un") | Some("--build-run") | Some("--patc") | Some("--emit-rus
          +t") | Some("--compare")) {                                    
      130 +        rest.remove(0);                                       
      131 +        if !rest.is_empty() { rest.remove(0); } // script path
      132 +    }                                                         
      133 +    Ok(Value::List(rest.into_iter().map(Value::String).collect
          +()))                                                          
      134 +}

      763 +                // The self-hosted compiler's back end: write 
          +source, run rustc.

          +st source string".into()) };

          +ng(s)) if !s.trim().is_empty() => s.clone(), _ => return Err("
          +rustc_build: expected output path".into()) };                 
      766 +                let mut tmp = std::env::temp_dir();

          +td::process::id()));

      216 +fn selfhost_fixpoint_patc_compiles_itself() {                 
      217 +    // The true fixpoint (step 2b):                           
      218 +    //   Gen A: the interpreter runs the PatLang compiler to c
          +ompile the                                                    
      219 +    //          compiler's own source into a native patc1     
      220 +    //   Gen B: patc1 compiles the feature demo (native compil
          +er works)                                                     
      221 +    //   Gen C: patc1 compiles its own source into patc2; patc
          +2 compiles the                                                
      222 +    //          feature demo with identical output            
      223 +    let rustc = std::env::var("RUSTC").unwrap_or_else(|_| "rus
          +tc".to_string());                                             
      224 +    if std::process::Command::new(&rustc).arg("--version").out
          +put().is_err() {                                              
      225 +        eprintln!("rustc not found; skipping fixpoint test"); 
      226 +        return;

      230 +    let repo_root = std::path::Path::new(manifest).parent().un
          +wrap().to_path_buf();                                         
      231 +    let read = |rel: &str| std::fs::read_to_string(repo_root.j
          +oin(rel)).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {}: {}", rel, e));  
      232 +                                                              
      233 +    // Concatenate the compiler's own source (same as build_pa
          +tc1.patlang)

      235 +        "{}\n{}\n{}\n{}\n{}",                                 
      236 +        read("self_hosting/lib/lexer.patlang"),

      240 +        read("self_hosting/patc1_main.patlang"),              
      241 +    );                                                        
      242 +                                                              
      243 +    let out_dir = std::env::temp_dir().join("patlang_fixpoint_
          +test");                                                       
      244 +    let _ = std::fs::create_dir_all(&out_dir);                
      245 +    let all_src_path = out_dir.join("patc1_all.patlang");     
      246 +    std::fs::write(&all_src_path, &compiler_src).expect("write
          + combined compiler source");                                  
      247 +    let exe = |n: &str| out_dir.join(if cfg!(windows) { format
          +!("{}.exe", n) } else { n.to_string() });                     
      248 +    let (patc1, patc2, demo_b, demo_c) = (exe("patc1"), exe("p
          +atc2"), exe("demo_b"), exe("demo_c"));                        
      249 +    let fwd = |p: &std::path::Path| p.display().to_string().re
          +place('\\', "/");                                             
      250 +                                                              
      251 +    // --- Gen A: interpreter runs the PatLang compiler on its
          + own source ---                                               
      252 +    let driver = format!(                                     
      253 +        "let source = read_file(\"{}\")\n\                    
      254 +         let toks = tokenize(source)\n\                       
      255 +         let ast = parse_program(toks)\n\                     
      256 +         let ir = lower_program(ast)\n\                       
      257 +         let rs = emit_program_rs(ir)\n\                      
      258 +         let exe = rustc_build(rs, \"{}\")\n\                 
      259 +         print(\"GEN A OK\")\n",                              
      260 +        fwd(&all_src_path), fwd(&patc1)                       
      261 +    );                                                        
      262 +    let gen_a_src = format!("{}\n{}", compiler_src, driver);  
      263 +    let mut parser = Stage0Parser::new(&gen_a_src).expect("lex
          +er init");                                                    
      264 +    let ast = parser.parse().expect("gen A source should parse
          +");                                                           
      265 +    let mut lower = Lowerer::new();                           
      266 +    let program = lower.lower_program_basic(&ast);            
      267 +    let mut interp = Interpreter::new();                      
      268 +    interp.host.insert("print", capture_print);               
      269 +    register_stage0_shims(&mut interp);                       
      270 +    PRINTED.with(|p| p.borrow_mut().clear());                 
      271 +    interp.run(&program).expect("gen A should run");          
      272 +    assert!(patc1.exists(), "gen A did not produce patc1");   
      273 +

      276 +        let st = std::process::Command::new(compiler)

          +e

● PatLang is now self-hosted in the strict sense — committed as b57c822 (stages
  5 and 6, d976cec + b57c822), with the tutorial updated at each step and all 49
  test suites green.

  What happened this session

  Stage 5 (d976cec): codegen moved into PatLang. The host's RustCodegen was
  split into a static prelude() (the runtime library text) and the dynamic
  program-builder section — and self_hosting/lib/codegen.patlang now generates
  that section itself, including Rust string escaping built from chr() so it
  stays inside the escape-free Stage 1 dialect. The host back end shrank to two
  trivial functions: hand over the prelude string, and rustc_build(source, out)
  — write a file, run rustc. pipeline_stage4.patlang runs lexer → parser →
  lowerer → codegen entirely in PatLang, generating ~78 KB of Rust. (This also
  flushed out a real bug: parallel tests were clobbering each other's temp
  source files — now unique per invocation.)

  Stage 6 (b57c822): the fixpoint. With argv/write_file hosts, a rustc_build arm
  inside compiled programs, and the prelude exported to
  self_hosting/runtime/prelude.rs (shipping the runtime as a file — the standard
  resolution of the quine problem), the three-generation bootstrap closed:

  - Gen A: the interpreter ran the PatLang compiler on the compiler's own 36 KB
  source (7,787 tokens, 41 functions) → native patc1.exe (~4 min)
  - Gen B: patc1.exe compiled the feature demo in 3.5 seconds — ~65× faster than
  interpreted — with correct output
  - Gen C: patc1.exe compiled its own source → patc2.exe, which compiles
  programs identically — and the two compilers emit byte-identical Rust for the
  same input

  Further development of the compiler can now happen in PatLang, compiled by
  PatLang. What remains outside the language is deliberate: the prelude file (a
  runtime-library artifact, regenerated by tools/dump_prelude.patlang) and rustc
  as the machine-code back end, used the way other compilers use LLVM. The full
  bootstrap is pinned by selfhost_fixpoint_patc_compiles_itself (#[ignore]d at
  ~7 min; run via cargo test --test selfhost_pipeline -- --ignored).

  Tutorial — updated at both steps: section 5 now shows the full-PatLang
  pipeline table and a "The fixpoint" walkthrough with the exact
  three-generation commands, patc1 usage, and how to regenerate the prelude.

  Worth knowing

  - Gen A's 4 minutes is dominated by O(n²) list_push in the interpreted
  tokenizer — the natural next optimization if bootstrap turnaround starts to
  matter.
  - The prelude file must be regenerated whenever the host template in
  codegen.rs changes — the tutorial and roadmap both note this.
  - Remaining language niceties on the roadmap: string escapes in the Stage 1
  lexer, Stage 1-level include (the compiler source is currently concatenated by
  the build script), real closures, and the async event loop.

✻ Worked for 25m 29s

※ recap: PatLang self-hosting is achieved: the compiler is written in PatLang and compiles itself natively with byte-identical output, all committed and tested. Next action: optimize the slow interpreted bootstrap or add remaining language niceties like string escapes and closures. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ We'd really like the prelude to also be compiled from patlang source if that  
  is reasonably achievable (obviously when bootstrapping on a new-to-patlang    
  system something has to be in another language to bootstrap, but to prove the 
  concept I think it needs to be end-to-end).                                   

● This is achievable, and there's a genuinely meaningful way to do it: port the
  prelude into PatLang source (lib/runtime_rs.patlang) as a structured
  runtime-text emitter — the same way real compiler codegens carry
  target-language templates — so every byte of an emitted program originates
  from .patlang files processed by the PatLang toolchain. The bootstrap seed
  then shrinks to rustc plus the Stage 0 interpreter used once for Generation A.

  Two obstacles first: the runtime source will roughly triple the compiler's
  size, and the current pipeline is O(n²) in two places (whole-list clones on
  every list_push/index, and chars().nth() scans in char_code). At 3× size
  that's the difference between minutes and hours, so performance comes first.

      | head -5)

     929:                    // output promptly — stdout is block-buffered
     when not a tty

      src/ir/codege…)
  ⎿  CLEAN

      176 +// Interned read-only strings: O(1) handle passing for hot loo
          +ps (the                                                       
      177 +// tokenizer indexes the whole source per character; a Value::
          +String local                                                  
      178 +// would be cloned on every access)                           
      179 +thread_local! {                                               
      180 +    static ISTRINGS: RefCell<Vec<String>> = RefCell::new(Vec::
          +new());

      182 +                                                              
      183 +pub fn host_str_intern(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String
          +> {                                                           
      184 +    let s = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
          +one(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() };  
      185 +    let id = ISTRINGS.with(|v| { let mut b = v.borrow_mut(); b
          +.push(s); b.len() - 1 });                                     
      186 +    Ok(Value::Number(id as f64))                              
      187 +}                                                             
      188 +                                                              
      189 +pub fn host_sc_len(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> { 
      190 +    let id = arg_usize(args, 0, "sc_len")?;

      193 +        b.get(id).ok_or_else(|| format!("sc_len: unknown strin
          +g {}", id))                                                   
      194 +            .map(|s| Value::Number(if s.is_ascii() { s.len() a
          +s f64 } else { s.chars().count() as f64 }))                   
      195 +    })                                                        
      196 +}

      199 +    let id = arg_usize(args, 0, "sc_code")?;

          +own string {}", id))?;                                        
      204 +        if s.is_ascii() {                                     
      205 +            return Ok(Value::Number(match s.as_bytes().get(idx
          +) { Some(c) => *c as f64, None => -1.0 }));

          + c as u32 as f64, None => -1.0 }))                            
      208 +    })

      287 -## 6. Debugging tips                                          
      287 +## 6. WebAssembly                                             
      288  
      289 +Because the compiler emits portable Rust, targeting WASM is on
          +e argument:                                                   
      290 +                                                              
      291 +```patlang                                                    
      292 +let wasm = rustc_build(rs, "./program.wasm", "wasm32-wasip1") 
      293 +```                                                           
      294 +                                                              
      295 +Setup (one-time): `rustup target add wasm32-wasip1`. Run the m
          +odule with any                                                
      296 +WASI runtime — with Node.js:                                  
      297 +                                                              
      298 +```js                                                         
      299 +// run_wasi.mjs                                               
      300 +import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';                  
      301 +import { WASI } from 'node:wasi';                             
      302 +import { argv, env } from 'node:process';                     
      303 +const wasi = new WASI({ version: 'preview1', args: argv.slice(
          +2), env });                                                   
      304 +const wasm = await WebAssembly.compile(await readFile(argv[2])
          +);                                                            
      305 +const instance = await WebAssembly.instantiate(wasm, wasi.getI
          +mportObject());                                               
      306 +wasi.start(instance);                                         
      307 +```                                                           
      308 +                                                              
      309 +```bash                                                       
      310 +node --no-warnings run_wasi.mjs program.wasm                  
      311 +```                                                           
      312 +                                                              
      313 +The feature demo (events, logic, OO, functional) runs on WASM 
          +with output                                                   
      314 +identical to native. Caveat: WASI preview1 has no sockets or s
          +ubprocesses,                                                  
      315 +so the `tcp_*` hosts and `rustc_build` fail at runtime there —
          + compute-,                                                    
      316 +event-, and file-oriented programs are the WASM sweet spot.   
      317 +                                                              
      318 +## 7. Browser GUI (HTML + JavaScript output)                  
      319 +                                                              
      320 +`self_hosting/lib/html.patlang` builds well-formed HTML5 pages
          + with embedded                                                
      321 +JavaScript, making the browser PatLang's cross-platform GUI. T
          +wo patterns:                                                  
      322 +                                                              
      323 +**Static page** — PatLang computes data and generates an inter
          +active page                                                   
      324 +(`self_hosting/examples/gui_demo.patlang`):                   
      325 +                                                              
      326 +```patlang                                                    
      327 +let page = html_page("My App", build_body(), build_script(data
          +))                                                            
      328 +write_file("app.html", page)                                  
      329 +```                                                           
      330 +                                                              
      331 +**Live backend** — a native PatLang server serves the page, an
          +d the page's                                                  
      332 +JS `fetch`es JSON computed live by the same process           
      333 +(`self_hosting/examples/gui_server_demo.patlang`):            
      334 +                                                              
      335 +```patlang                                                    
      336 +let port = tcp_listen(0)                                      
      337 +# GET /      -> html_page(...) via http_ok("text/html", ...)  
      338 +# GET /data  -> live JSON via http_ok("application/json", ...)
      339 +```                                                           
      340 +                                                              
      341 +Tip: use single quotes inside embedded JS/CSS/HTML attributes 
          +— the Stage 1                                                 
      342 +dialect has no string escapes, and single-quoted JS needs none
          +. `q()` gives a                                               
      343 +double quote where one is unavoidable (e.g. JSON keys).       
      344 +                                                              
      345 +## 8. Debugging tips                                          
      346 +                                                              
      347  - `PATLANG_DEBUG=1` enables evaluator/parser debug logs.
      348  - `pat --emit-rust file.patlang` prints the generated Rust for
            a program —
      349    useful when a compiled binary misbehaves.
