  Wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

● All four work items are done and committed (5df646c, d8aaa68, b3fd893), with
  the tutorial updated at each step and all 50 test suites green.

  End-to-end prelude (your main ask) — done

  The runtime library embedded in every emitted program is now PatLang source.
  self_hosting/lib/runtime_rs.patlang emits the runtime text, generated once
  from the host template by tools/transcribe_prelude.py and pinned byte-for-byte
  to it by a new parity test — so drift between the two is caught immediately.
  emit_program_rs uses it and patc1 no longer reads any prelude file: every byte
  of an emitted program originates from .patlang files processed by the PatLang
  toolchain. I re-ran the full three-generation fixpoint with the runtime
  riding inside the compiler: it holds, with byte-identical emission between
  patc1 and patc2. The bootstrap seed on a fresh machine is now exactly what you
  allowed for — rustc as the machine back end, plus one Generation-A run of the
  Stage 0 interpreter.

  Getting there required making the pipeline O(n) first (stage 7):
  value-semantics locals were cloning entire token lists and source strings on
  every access, so I added handle-based builders (vec_*, sb_*, interned strings)
  to both VMs and rewrote the compiler libs onto them. Generation A went from
  3m51s to 45s; with the runtime aboard, self-compilation is ~7 minutes, now
  dominated by rustc digesting the 900KB emitted compiler.

  WASM — works, unmodified

  Since the compiler emits portable Rust, rustc_build just needed an optional
  target triple. With wasm32-wasip1 installed, the PatLang pipeline
  cross-compiled the feature demo to .wasm with zero source changes, and it runs
  under Node's WASI with output identical to native — events, logic, OO, and
  functional all intact. Caveat documented: WASI preview1 has no sockets or
  subprocesses, so tcp_* and rustc_build are native-only for now.

  Browser GUI — two working patterns

  self_hosting/lib/html.patlang builds well-formed HTML5 pages with embedded
  JavaScript (doctype, charset, viewport, balanced tags — all test-asserted).
  The dialect-friendly trick: single-quoted JS needs no escapes, and q() covers
  JSON's double quotes.

  - gui_demo.patlang: native PatLang computes a fib table and generates an
  interactive filterable page via write_file.
  - gui_server_demo.patlang: the compelling one — a native PatLang server serves
  the page at /, and the page's JavaScript fetches /data, which returns JSON
  computed live by the same PatLang process. Verified over real HTTP: {"source":
  "native patlang", "fib": [0, 1, 1, 2, ...]}.

  Tutorial gained sections 6 (WebAssembly, with a ready-to-use Node WASI runner)
  and 7 (Browser GUI, both patterns).

  Worth knowing: the regeneration flow after any host-template change is now two
  commands (dump_prelude.patlang, then transcribe_prelude.py) — the parity test
  fails loudly if forgotten. Natural next steps if you want them: an emit-based
  async event loop over the TCP hosts (would let the GUI server handle
  concurrent browsers), shrinking rustc's share of self-compile time, and string
  escapes in the Stage 1 lexer.

      278 +pub fn host_now_ms(_args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> {
      279 +    // now_ms() -> Number: milliseconds since the Unix epoch (
          +monotonic-enough                                              
      280 +    // for self-timing; under the browser WASI shim it maps to
          + performance.now)                                             
      281 +    let ms = std::time::SystemTime::now()                     
      282 +        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)                
      283 +        .map(|d| d.as_millis() as f64)                        
      284 +        .unwrap_or(0.0);                                      
      285 +    Ok(Value::Number(ms))                                     
      286 +}                                                             
      287 +                                                              
      288 +pub fn host_read_file_b64(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Str
          +ing> {                                                        
      289 +    // read_file_b64(path) -> base64 of the raw bytes (for emb
          +edding binary                                                 
      290 +    // artifacts like .wasm in generated HTML). Builder-side h
          +ost.                                                          
      291 +    let p = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
          +one(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() };  
      292 +    let data = std::fs::read(&p).map_err(|e| format!("read_fil
          +e_b64: {}: {}", p, e))?;                                      
      293 +    const TBL: &[u8; 64] = b"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefg
          +hijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/";                             
      294 +    let mut out = String::with_capacity((data.len() + 2) / 3 *
          + 4);                                                          
      295 +    for chunk in data.chunks(3) {                             
      296 +        let b = [chunk[0], *chunk.get(1).unwrap_or(&0), *chunk
          +.get(2).unwrap_or(&0)];                                       
      297 +        let n = ((b[0] as u32) << 16) | ((b[1] as u32) << 8) |
          + b[2] as u32;                                                 
      298 +        out.push(TBL[(n >> 18) as usize & 63] as char);       
      299 +        out.push(TBL[(n >> 12) as usize & 63] as char);       
      300 +        out.push(if chunk.len() > 1 { TBL[(n >> 6) as usize & 
          +63] as char } else { '=' });                                  
      301 +        out.push(if chunk.len() > 2 { TBL[n as usize & 63] as 
          +char } else { '=' });                                         
      302 +    }                                                         
      303 +    Ok(Value::String(out))                                    
      304 +}                                                             
      305 +                                                              
      306 +pub fn host_exec_capture(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Stri
          +ng> {                                                         
      307 +    // exec_capture(path) -> stdout of running the program (no
          + arguments).                                                  
      308 +    // Builder-side host for capturing native transcripts of c
          +ompiled demos.                                                
      309 +    let p = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
          +one(), _ => return Err("exec_capture: expected program path".i
          +nto()) };                                                     
      310 +    let out = std::process::Command::new(&p).output().map_err(
          +|e| format!("exec_capture: {}: {}", p, e))?;                  
      311 +    Ok(Value::String(String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).to_s
          +tring()))                                                     
      312 +}

  Wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

● Yes, it was worth it — and it's built, committed as eea2341, with all 50 test
  suites green.

  What you got

  portfolio/index.html — a single self-contained file (no network, works from
  file://). Open it in any browser. Each example card shows:

  - The PatLang source (collapsible, syntax-safe escaped)
  - A "Run in this browser" button — the program, compiled to wasm32-wasip1 by
  the self-hosted compiler, embedded as base64 and executed via an inline
  hand-rolled WASI shim (~40 lines of JS covering fd_write, clock_time_get,
  random_get, etc., with a Proxy fallback so unlisted imports degrade gracefully
  instead of link-erroring)
  - Performance metrics from both sides: programs time themselves with the new
  now_ms() built-in, so the same self-reported numbers appear in the browser run
  and in the native transcript captured on the build machine at generation time

  The cards: the all-paradigms feature demo, a dedicated self-timing benchmark
  (fib_bench.patlang), the GUI generator (source + native transcript, since it
  writes files), and a "compiler measured on itself" card — the front end's
  token/statement/function counts and per-phase timings, measured live on its
  own complete source while the page was being built.

  The generator is itself the best exhibit:
  self_hosting/tools/build_portfolio.patlang is a PatLang program that compiles
  the demos to native and WASM, runs the native binaries to capture transcripts,
  base64-embeds the WASM, and emits the page with lib/html.patlang — the
  showcase is produced end-to-end by the thing it showcases. Regeneration is one
  command (documented in portfolio/README.md and tutorial §8).

  Verified headlessly: since I can't open a browser here, the smoke test
  extracts the page's actual <script> block and runs it in Node with a DOM stub
  — the embedded shim + WASM produce correct output. The numbers came out
  interesting: fib(27) at ~161ms in browser WASM vs ~195ms native vs ~975ms 
  interpreted (the wall-clock line each card shows separately includes WASM
  compile time, ~700ms).

  Two caveats worth knowing: the committed page is ~5.5MB (two embedded WASM
  modules; -C strip/opt-level=s would shrink them — noted on the roadmap), and
  if you later change the codegen template, the usual two-command prelude
  regeneration also refreshes what the portfolio embeds. A natural extension
  when you want it: hosting the portfolio from the PatLang GUI server so the
  whole demo stack — page, server, compiler — is one PatLang binary.

❯ OK let's work on the Curent Limitations in the Tutorial file                  

● Reading the current limitations list, they rank like this: string escapes and
  non-short-circuit and/or are real warts with bounded fixes; async has a
  meaningful middle step (a JS-style single-threaded event loop over the event
  system — philosophically consistent with the when/emit design); closures are a
  major IR project best left scoped on the roadmap; per-thread state is a
  non-issue until the language has threads. I'll fix the first three.

  Starting with escapes in the Stage 1 lexer (matching Stage 0 semantics
  exactly: \n \t \r \" \\ decode, unknown escapes stay raw):

      365  
      366  ## 10. Current limitations
      367  
      368 -- The self-hosted (Stage 1) dialect has no string escape seque
          -nces yet — use                                                
      369 -  `chr(code)` to build special characters (`chr(10)` = newline
          -).                                                            
      370 -- `and` / `or` do not short-circuit in code compiled through t
          -he Stage 1                                                    
      371 -  pipeline (both operands evaluate).                          
      372 -- Objects, facts, and event handlers live in per-thread state.
      373 -- Functions are not yet first-class closures; use `apply(name,
          - ...)`.                                                       
      374 -- Networking is blocking; the async/event-loop model is on the
          - roadmap                                                      
      375 -  (`PATLANG_SELF_HOSTING_ROADMAP.md`).                        
      368 +- Functions are not yet first-class closures; pass function na
          +mes and invoke                                                
      369 +  with `apply(name, ...)`. Lexical closures are the next major
          + IR feature                                                   
      370 +  (see `PATLANG_SELF_HOSTING_ROADMAP.md`).                    
      371 +- Concurrency is cooperative: the event loop (section 4, Async
          +) is                                                          
      372 +  single-threaded like JavaScript's. There are no OS threads i
          +n the language                                                
      373 +  yet — which is also why the object/fact/event-handler stores
          + being                                                        
      374 +  per-thread is unobservable in practice.                     
      375 +- Compiling is fast; *self*-compiling takes minutes because ru
          +stc digests the                                               
      376 +  ~1 MB emitted compiler. `patc` output is unoptimized for siz
          +e (WASM modules                                               
      377 +  ~2 MB); `strip`/`opt-level=s` support is on the roadmap.    
      378 +                                                              
      379 +Fixed since earlier versions: string escapes (`\n \t \r \" \\`
          +) now work in                                                 
      380 +the Stage 1 dialect, and `and`/`or` short-circuit in Stage-1-c
          +ompiled code                                                  
      381 +exactly as in Stage 0.                                        

● Update(TUTORIAL.md)
  ⎿  Added 35 lines
      198  pat --ir-run self_hosting/pipeline_stage2.patlang   # or compi
           le echo_server directly
      199  ```
      200  
      201 +### Async: the event loop                                     
      202 +                                                              
      203 +Concurrency is event-driven and single-threaded, like JavaScri
          +pt: a loop                                                    
      204 +turns external happenings into events, and `when` handlers con
          +sume them.                                                    
      205 +`tcp_accept_timeout(port, ms)` is the non-blocking primitive (
          +returns -1 on                                                 
      206 +timeout), `sleep_ms(ms)` yields time, and shared state lives i
          +n the object                                                  
      207 +store via `set_var`/`get`:                                    
      208 +                                                              
      209 +```patlang                                                    
      210 +when request do                                               
      211 +  # event_data is the connection id                           
      212 +  let text = tcp_read(event_data)                             
      213 +  tcp_write(event_data, "...")                                
      214 +  tcp_close(event_data)                                       
      215 +end                                                           
      216 +                                                              
      217 +when tick do                                                  
      218 +  set_var("ticks", get("__vars", "ticks") + 1)                
      219 +end                                                           
      220 +                                                              
      221 +set_var("ticks", 0)                                           
      222 +let port = tcp_listen(0)                                      
      223 +while running do                                              
      224 +  let conn = tcp_accept_timeout(port, 50)                     
      225 +  if conn >= 0 then                                           
      226 +    emit("request", conn)                                     
      227 +  else                                                        
      228 +    emit("tick", 0)                                           
      229 +  end                                                         
      230 +end

  Wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

● Done — committed as e7dceb0, with the tutorial's Current Limitations list now
  mostly retired and all 50 test suites green.

  Three limitations fixed

  String escapes in the Stage 1 dialect. The self-hosted lexer now decodes \n \t
  \r \" \\ (unknown escapes stay raw), matching Stage 0's semantics exactly. No
  more chr(10) gymnastics for everyday strings — the event-loop example below
  writes its HTTP headers as plain "\r\n".

  Short-circuit and/or. The Stage 1 lowerer now emits the same jump-patched
  short-circuit sequences as the Stage 0 lowerer, so the two dialects finally
  agree on evaluation semantics. Verified the strong way: false and 
  missing_fn(1) compiles and runs without ever calling the nonexistent function
  (it would have aborted with "host fn not found" under the old eager
  evaluation).

  Async. PatLang now has a concurrency story: a single-threaded event loop,
  JavaScript-style, built on the machinery the language already had. Two new
  primitives in both VMs — tcp_accept_timeout(port, ms) (returns -1 on timeout)
  and sleep_ms(ms) — let a plain while loop turn connections into request events
  and idle time into tick events, dispatched to when handlers with shared state
  in the object store. examples/event_loop_server.patlang compiles through the
  self-hosted pipeline to a native exe that served two live curl requests while
  counting 30 idle ticks. The tutorial gained an "Async: the event loop" section
  with the pattern.

  All of it is pinned by a new regression test
  (stage1_escapes_and_short_circuit) that goes through the full PatLang pipeline
  to a native binary, and the prelude artifacts were regenerated for the new
  host arms.

  What the limitations section says now

  The rewritten list is down to the honest residue: closures (the next major IR
  feature — apply(name, ...) remains the workaround), cooperative-only 
  concurrency (no OS threads — which is also why per-thread state is
  unobservable in practice, so I reframed that former bullet), and build 
  sizes/times (rustc chewing ~1MB of emitted compiler source; unstripped ~2MB
  WASM modules).

  One housekeeping note: during the full-suite run the echo-server test flaked
  once under parallel load (a Windows RST race truncating the client read —
  headers arrived, body didn't). It passes reliably in isolation and didn't
  reproduce on the follow-up full run; I've noted it in my project memory with
  the fix to apply (client-side retry in the test) if it recurs. Also, git
  printed a harmless multi-pack-index: Permission denied warning during commit —
  repack maintenance on the F: drive, the commits themselves are fine.

  Natural next target if you want to keep going: closures — lambda-lifting
  anonymous functions with captured environments into the existing apply
  machinery would be the incremental path before real IR-level environments.

● All built, tested, and committed as 5ce6855 — the portfolio now has four new
  cards, and all 50 test suites stay green.

  The bigger example: point of sale

  lib/pos.patlang + examples/pos_demo.patlang — a checkout session as a stream
  of scan/pay events, deliberately weaving three paradigms together: the product
  catalog lives in the object store (new/send/get), a dairy fact drives the
  10%-off rule (query at scan time — milk rings up 108p instead of 120p), and
  the till is pure events with when handlers. It handles unknown items
  gracefully and prints a receipt with change. On the portfolio it runs
  in-browser in ~28ms, next to its native transcript.

  The dev tools

  Test framework (lib/test.patlang): unit assertions (check, pass/fail counting,
  t_report) plus a Gherkin runner — step definitions register in the object
  store keyed by their text, and the runner parses
  Feature/Scenario/Given/When/Then/And lines from a plain string and dispatches
  through apply. examples/pos_tests.patlang exercises the POS all three ways —
  unit checks on pricing and facts, integration through emitted events, and a
  five-step Gherkin scenario — 10/10 passing, both natively and running live in 
  the browser card.

  patbuild (tools/patbuild_main.patlang): a goal-oriented build system — targets
  are objects, dependencies are walked recursively, completed builds become
  facts (queried for the final artifact count), progress is reported via built
  events, and each target is compiled natively by the self-hosted pipeline.
  Since the compiled patbuild.exe embeds the entire compiler, its portfolio
  transcript is a PatLang binary building PatLang programs.

  flowgraph (tools/flowgraph_main.patlang): real compiler tooling — it lowers a
  program with the self-hosted compiler, recovers basic blocks and jump edges
  from the IR, and renders SVG control-flow graphs (red curves for conditional
  jumps, blue for unconditional) into portfolio/flowgraph.html — 3 functions, 12
  blocks for the benchmark demo, linked from its portfolio card.

  Language fixes shaken out along the way

  Writing real programs exposed three Stage 0 parser gaps, now fixed:
  goal(...)/rule(...) were being silently skipped inside function bodies
  (statement-starter lists didn't include the keyword tokens), the when EVENT do
  ... end form only worked through the Stage 1 pipeline (Stage 0 only knew the
  brace form — and worse, swallowed the rest of the file), and unary minus (-1)
  wasn't parseable in expression position. This is the dogfooding dividend —
  each tool made the language better.

  The portfolio (index.html, now ~11MB with four embedded WASM modules) and
  tutorial §8 are updated. Worth noting: the rebuild took ~9 minutes, almost all
  rustc compiling patbuild's embedded compiler — reinforcing that emitted-code
  size/speed is the next infrastructure target if the portfolio grows. Natural
  follow-ons: patbuild reading a real manifest file instead of an inline one,
  the test framework gaining run_feature_file + tags, and flowgraph taking its
  input from argv to become a general CLI tool.

● Done — committed as 98eae17, all 50 test suites green, portfolio rebuilt and
  headlessly verified.

  What changed

  Test framework grew tags and feature files. run_feature_tagged(feature, 
  "@smoke") skips scenarios whose preceding @tag line doesn't match —
  demonstrated live in the portfolio's in-browser card, where the @smoke @till
  checkout scenario runs while the @audit scenario (which would fail) shows
  [skipped: needs @smoke], keeping the suite at 10/10. run_feature_file(path)
  loads .feature files directly. The feature text in pos_tests is now a readable
  multi-line string rather than escape soup.

  patbuild is manifest-driven. Targets come from self_hosting/patbuild.manifest
  (name <source|-> [deps...], # comments), with argv overrides for both the
  manifest path and the goal target — so the compiled patbuild.exe is a genuine
  reusable CLI: patbuild.exe mymanifest all. Its portfolio card now displays the
  manifest alongside the build transcript (3 targets parsed, dependency-ordered
  build, artifact count from facts).

  flowgraph is a CLI too: flowgraph <input.patlang> <output.html> — verified by
  pointing it at a different program with a custom output path.

  Portfolio coverage: all four of the build/test/flowgraph/POS items are on the
  page — the POS and the test framework as run-in-your-browser WASM cards (the
  framework genuinely executes its 10 assertions plus the tag-skip in the
  visitor's browser), and patbuild/flowgraph as native cards with sources,
  manifest, transcripts, and the link to the generated SVG flow graphs — since
  those two need rustc and the filesystem, which the browser sandbox doesn't
  offer.

  Two operational notes: the portfolio rebuild takes ~10 minutes (rustc
  compiling patbuild's embedded compiler dominates — caching unchanged demo
  builds in the builder would cut most of it), and the recurring
  multi-pack-index: Permission denied git warning is still just the F:-drive
  repack hiccup; commits are unaffected, though a git maintenance run from an
  elevated shell would silence it. Natural next steps: build caching in patbuild
  (needs a file-mtime host), and letting flowgraph render whole multi-function
  programs with inter-procedural call edges.

● Done — the live playground is in the portfolio, committed as b53c587, with all
  50 test suites green.

  How it works

  The insight that made it feasible: rustc can't run in a browser, but it
  doesn't have to. The playground WASM module contains the self-hosted lexer + 
  parser + lowerer (no codegen), and a new run_ir(ir_shape) host — added to both
  VMs — decodes the freshly lowered IR and executes it directly on the
  runtime's VM. So the pipeline in the visitor's browser is: textarea → WASI
  argv → PatLang tokenizer → PatLang parser (with parse errors surfaced) →
  PatLang lowerer → VM execution → stdout back into the page.

  The editor is a syntax-highlighted overlay (highlighted <pre> behind a
  transparent-text textarea, scroll-synced) — keywords, strings, numbers, and
  comments colored by a small char-walking highlighter, with an editable default
  program showing functions, recursion, lists, a while loop, and a when/emit
  event.

  Verified headlessly: the smoke test evaluated the actual page script and fed
  it a program defining and calling a new function — output live: 42 / 42 in
  29.3ms wall, including compiling the user's code. The highlighter was
  exercised too (let/string/comment/number all classified correctly).

  Supporting work: the WASI shim now implements real args_sizes_get/args_get
  (encoding the editor text as program arguments — which also means any future
  portfolio card can take inputs), and the prelude artifacts were regenerated
  for the new run_ir arm with the parity test confirming sync.

  Two notes: the page is now ~14MB (five embedded WASM modules — the module-size
  and rebuild-time items on the roadmap are getting more valuable; the rebuild
  is up to ~12 minutes, so build caching in the portfolio builder is probably
  the next quality-of-life win). And one honest limitation to be aware of: the
  playground compiles with the Stage 1 dialect, so anything the self-hosted
  grammar doesn't cover yet (closures, the aspirational DSL forms) will show as
  parse errors in the output pane — arguably a feature, since it documents
  exactly where the language stands.

  > App execution aliases.

  generate a PatLang section — end-to-end in PatLang, per your purity request —
  published into D:\Projects_Organized\parslow-soft-editorial\publish\patlang\,

  handled), and copies in the portfolio and flow-graph pages. Both docs pages
  were regenerated after the portfolio shrank, so the copy there now matches
  byte-for-byte.

  One language wrinkle surfaced while writing the generator, worth knowing:
  PatLang function bodies can't see top-level/caller locals — everything has to
  come in as a parameter (an output-path variable silently read as empty inside
  a helper until I passed it explicitly). Noted in project memory in case it
  bites again.

  into the site pages too, and consider build caching for the portfolio (still

  cadence picks up.

✻ Baked for 3m 0s

❯ Ah yes just remembered something I think is missing at the moment - PatLang   
  should have enforceable contracts on                                          
    functions (well, on anything really). Given rustc seems to be such a drag   
  on timing, is it worth considering if a                                       
    native PatLang approach might be better for this?  But wiring in the        
  flowgraph and buildtools to the portfolio makes a lot of sense too            

● Both are excellent points. Let me address them together, since they're
  related: contracts are a VM/IR-level semantic, not a codegen concern — they
  should work identically whether a program runs interpreted, via the no-rustc
  run_ir path (the same one powering the browser playground), or compiled
  through rustc. That's the "native PatLang approach" — checking happens in the
  VM itself, so rustc's slowness is irrelevant to contract checking, only to the
  final native-binary step which is unaffected by adding this feature. I'll
  prove that explicitly by demonstrating a contract violation through the
  rustc-free path.

  Design: require EXPR / ensure EXPR (plus a standalone assert EXPR usable
  anywhere) as new statement forms, lowered identically to a
  contract_check(func_name, kind, text, ok) host call in both the Stage 0 Rust
  compiler and the self-hosted PatLang compiler, backed by one host arm shared
  across both VMs.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

      312          }

      315 +                                                              
      316 +/// Render an expression back to readable source text, for con
          +tract-violation                                               
      317 +/// messages ("require b != 0" should report "b != 0", not a d
          +ebug dump).                                                   
      318 +pub fn expr_to_text(e: &Expr) -> String {                     
      319 +    match e {                                                 
      320 +        Expr::Number(n) => if n.fract() == 0.0 { format!("{}",
          + *n as i64) } else { n.to_string() },                         
      321 +        Expr::String(s) => format!("\"{}\"", s),              
      322 +        Expr::Identifier(name) => name.clone(),               
      323 +        Expr::List(items) => format!("[{}]", items.iter().map(
          +expr_to_text).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),                
      324 +        Expr::Member { object, property } => format!("{}.{}", 
          +expr_to_text(object), property),                              
      325 +        Expr::Index { object, index } => format!("{}[{}]", exp
          +r_to_text(object), expr_to_text(index)),                      
      326 +        Expr::Closure { .. } => "<closure>".into(),           
      327 +        Expr::UnaryOp { op, expr } => format!("{}{}", op, expr
          +_to_text(expr)),                                              
      328 +        Expr::BinaryOp { left, op, right } => {               
      329 +            let sym = match op {                              
      330 +                BinaryOperator::Add => "+", BinaryOperator::Su
          +b => "-",                                                     
      331 +                BinaryOperator::Mul => "*", BinaryOperator::Di
          +v => "/", BinaryOperator::Mod => "%",                         
      332 +                BinaryOperator::And => "and", BinaryOperator::
          +Or => "or",                                                   
      333 +                BinaryOperator::Equal => "==", BinaryOperator:
          +:NotEqual => "!=",                                            
      334 +                BinaryOperator::Greater => ">", BinaryOperator
          +::GreaterEqual => ">=",                                       
      335 +                BinaryOperator::Less => "<", BinaryOperator::L
          +essEqual => "<=",                                             
      336 +            };                                                
      337 +            format!("{} {} {}", expr_to_text(left), sym, expr_
          +to_text(right))                                               
      338 +        }                                                     
      339 +        Expr::Call { function, args } => format!("{}({})", exp
          +r_to_text(function), args.iter().map(expr_to_text).collect::<V
          +ec<_>>().join(", ")),                                         
      340 +    }                                                         
      341 +}

  ⎿  Added 24 lines
       996      Ok(Value::Unit)

       998  
       999 +/// contract_check(func_name, kind, text, ok) — the shared de
           +sign-by-contract                                             
      1000 +/// enforcement primitive. `kind` is "require" | "ensure" | "
           +assert". This is a                                           
      1001 +/// VM-level semantic: it behaves identically whether the pro
           +gram is                                                      
      1002 +/// interpreted, executed via run_ir (no rustc — e.g. the bro
           +wser playground),                                            
      1003 +/// or compiled natively, because the same logic is duplicate
           +d verbatim into                                              
      1004 +/// the codegen template's host arm.                         
      1005 +pub fn host_contract_check(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, S
           +tring> {                                                     
      1006 +    if args.len() != 4 { return Err("contract_check: expected
           + 4 args".into()); }                                          
      1007 +    let func = match &args[0] { Value::String(s) => s.clone()
           +, _ => String::new() };                                      
      1008 +    let kind = match &args[1] { Value::String(s) => s.clone()
           +, _ => String::new() };                                      
      1009 +    let text = match &args[2] { Value::String(s) => s.clone()
           +, _ => String::new() };                                      
      1010 +    let ok = args[3].as_bool().unwrap_or(false);             
      1011 +    if ok {                                                  
      1012 +        Ok(Value::Unit)                                      
      1013 +    } else {                                                 
      1014 +        let label = match kind.as_str() {                    
      1015 +            "require" => "precondition",                     
      1016 +            "ensure" => "postcondition",                     
      1017 +            _ => "assertion",                                
      1018 +        };                                                   
      1019 +        Err(format!("contract violation: {} failed in {}(): {
           +}", label, func, text))                                      
      1020 +    }                                                        
      1021 +}                                                            
      1022 +                                                             
      1023  fn playground_print(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String> 
            {
      1024      if let Some(v) = args.get(0) {
      1025          println!("{}", display_value(v));

      237  
      238  `read_file(path)` returns a file's contents as a string.
      239  
      240 -## 5. The self-hosting pipeline                               
      240 +## 5. Design by contract                                      
      241  
      242 +`require EXPR` checks a precondition, `ensure EXPR` a postcond
          +ition, and                                                    
      243 +`assert EXPR` is the same primitive usable standalone anywhere
          + (including                                                   
      244 +outside any function). All three lower to one `contract_check(
          +func, kind,                                                   
      245 +text, ok)` call: if the condition is false, execution aborts w
          +ith a message                                                 
      246 +naming the function, the kind of check, and the condition itse
          +lf.                                                           
      247 +                                                              
      248 +```patlang                                                    
      249 +make a function called safe_divide takes a, b returns r       
      250 +  require b != 0                                              
      251 +  let r = a / b                                               
      252 +  ensure (r * b) == a                                         
      253 +  return r                                                    
      254 +end                                                           
      255 +                                                              
      256 +print(safe_divide(10, 2))   # 5                               
      257 +assert (1 + 1) == 2         # standalone check, passes silentl
          +y                                                             
      258 +print(safe_divide(1, 0))    # aborts: contract violation: prec
          +ondition                                                      
      259 +                             # failed in safe_divide(): b != 0
      260 +```                                                           
      261 +                                                              
      262 +`ensure` is checked wherever it's written — typically right be
          +fore the                                                      
      263 +value it names is returned — so functions with multiple exit p
          +aths just                                                     
      264 +repeat `ensure` before each `return` they want checked; there'
          +s no implicit                                                 
      265 +"check at every exit" magic.                                  
      266 +                                                              
      267 +This is a VM-level semantic, not a codegen concern: it works i
          +dentically                                                    
      268 +whether the program is interpreted, executed via `run_ir` (no 
          +rustc — the                                                   
      269 +same path the browser playground uses), or compiled natively, 
          +because the                                                   
      270 +same `contract_check` logic is duplicated verbatim into the co
          +degen                                                         
      271 +template. Enforcing contracts costs nothing extra at the rustc
          + step — it's                                                  
      272 +one ordinary host call, like any other. See                   
      273 +`self_hosting/examples/contracts_demo.patlang` (also on the po
          +rtfolio,                                                      
      274 +runnable in-browser) for a fuller example, including a recursi
          +ve function                                                   
      275 +(`factorial`) with both a precondition and a postcondition.   
      276 +                                                              
      277 +## 6. The self-hosting pipeline                               
      278 +                                                              
      279  PatLang's compiler is written in PatLang, end to end:
      280  
      281  | Component | File | Written in |

       3  This document outlines the pragmatic next steps to reach self-h

       4  
       5 +## status update (July 4, 2026): design by contract + dev-tool 
         +portfolio wiring                                               
       6 +                                                               
       7 +**Design by contract** (`require`/`ensure`/`assert`) landed as 
         +a genuine                                                      
       8 +VM-level semantic, not a codegen feature — deliberately, since 
         +rustc is the                                                   
       9 +slow part of this stack and contract *checking* has nothing to 
         +do with it:                                                    
      10 +                                                               
      11 +- New `Stmt::Assert { kind, expr }` in both the Stage 0 Rust AS
         +T/parser/                                                      
      12 +  lowerer and the Stage 1 self-hosted parser/lowerer (`self_hos
         +ting/lib/                                                      
      13 +  parser.patlang` reuses its own `ast_to_str` to render violati
         +on messages;                                                   
      14 +  `lower.patlang` threads a `fname` parameter through `lower_bl
         +ock`/                                                          
      15 +  `lower_stmt` for "which function" context).                  
      16 +- One shared `contract_check(func_name, kind, text, ok)` host a
         +rm, present                                                    
      17 +  verbatim in both the interpreter (`ir/hosts.rs`) and the emit
         +ted-program                                                    
      18 +  codegen template (`ir/codegen.rs`) — so enforcement is byte-f
         +or-byte                                                        
      19 +  identical whether a program is interpreted, executed via `run
         +_ir` (no                                                       
      20 +  rustc — the browser-playground path), or compiled natively. P
         +roved this                                                     
      21 +  explicitly: a precondition violation was demonstrated through
         + all three                                                     
      22 +  paths before anything else was built on top.                 
      23 +- `ensure` is checked wherever it's written (not wrapped around
         + every                                                         
      24 +  `return`), so multi-exit functions just repeat `ensure` befor
         +e whichever                                                    
      25 +  exits they want checked — no hidden control-flow rewriting.  
      26 +- `self_hosting/examples/contracts_demo.patlang`: passing requi
         +re/ensure/                                                     
      27 +  assert on `safe_divide`/`clamp`/`factorial`, then one deliber
         +ate                                                            
      28 +  precondition violation, so the demo shows enforcement actuall
         +y firing.                                                      
      29 +- `host_exec_capture` now appends stderr when the process exits
         + non-zero,                                                     
      30 +  so native transcripts of intentionally-failing demos show the
         + violation                                                     
      31 +  message instead of silently truncating.                      
      32 +- Found and fixed one non-ASCII character (an em dash in a new 
         +codegen                                                        
      33 +  comment) that broke the prelude byte-parity test — a reminder
         + that the                                                      
      34 +  "prelude must be pure ASCII" invariant from the performance w
         +ork                                                            
      35 +  (stage 7) is easy to violate accidentally and is caught immed
         +iately by                                                      
      36 +  `selfhost_runtime_text_parity`.                              
      37 +                                                               
      38 +**Dev tools wired more deeply into the portfolio:**            
      39 +- `flowgraph` now renders *multiple* programs into one navigabl
         +e page                                                         
      40 +  (`<flowgraph <output.html> [inputs...]>`; with no arguments i
         +t renders a                                                    
      41 +  curated set — the benchmark, the contracts demo, the point-of
         +-sale                                                          
      42 +  bundle, and the all-paradigms demo). Rendering IR doesn't req
         +uire                                                           
      43 +  semantic resolution, so a library+driver pair can be bundled 
         +with a                                                         
      44 +  `lib.patlang+demo.patlang` spec without needing a real module
         + system.

● Everything is verified, committed, and documented. Here's the summary:

  Design by contract — landed as a pure VM semantic

  Directly addressing your question: contracts have nothing to do with rustc.
  require/ensure/assert all lower to one shared contract_check(func, kind, text,
  ok) host call, present verbatim in the interpreter and the compiled-program
  template. I proved this concretely with a regression test that drives the
  self-hosted lexer/parser/lowerer and executes the result via run_ir with zero 
  rustc invocations — the same path the browser playground uses. Enforcement is
  identical whether interpreted, run in-browser, or compiled natively; rustc's
  slowness only ever affected the compile step, never checking.

  ensure is checked wherever it's written (not auto-wrapped around every
  return), so multi-exit functions just repeat it before whichever exits matter.
  contracts_demo.patlang shows passing checks on safe_divide/clamp/factorial
  then one deliberate violation — now a live portfolio card, runnable in your
  browser.

  Flowgraph and patbuild, wired deeper

  flowgraph now renders multiple programs into one navigable page (curated by
  default: the benchmark, the contracts demo, the point-of-sale bundle, and the
  all-paradigms demo), and patbuild.manifest has real targets for the new demos
  — both using a lib.patlang+demo.patlang bundling convention I added since
  rendering IR doesn't require a real module system.

  A real bug found along the way

  Writing the curated flowgraph list across multiple lines (for readability)
  exposed that the Stage 1 self-hosted parser's list-literal and call-argument
  loops never skipped newline tokens — multi-line lists and function calls were
  silently mis-parsing into empty values with no error surfaced at all (the
  malformed node fell through a catch-all). Fixed by adding skip_nl calls
  matching what the Stage 0 Rust parser already tolerates, with a regression
  test locking it in. Worth remembering: the two parsers can drift when a
  tolerance is only patched on one side.

  All 51 test suites green, portfolio and site rebuilt and headlessly verified
  (contracts card and flowgraph confirmed correct in-browser), everything
  committed as 4f87259.

● This is substantial — the parser already accepts |params| { body } and
  trailing-block closures, but the lowerer treats every closure as a no-op
  (<closure> placeholder), and there's no Value::Closure or indirect-call
  instruction at all. Real closures need: a closure value carrying captured
  variables, free-variable analysis at the closure-literal site, and a new "call
  this value" instruction. Let me scope the work.

      340 +                                                              
      341 +    /// Lowers a closure literal: analyzes free variables, syn
          +thesizes a                                                    
      342 +    /// Function (params = captured names ++ the closure's own
          + params, in                                                   
      343 +    /// that order) queued in `pending_closures`, and emits th
          +e                                                             
      344 +    /// LoadLocal.../MakeClosure sequence at the creation site
          + in `f`.                                                      
      345 +    fn lower_closure_literal(&mut self, params: &[String], bod
          +y: &[Stmt], f: &mut Function) {                               
      346 +        // This language has no block scoping (reassigning a l
          +ocal inside an                                                
      347 +        // if/while branch is already visible after it, everyw
          +here else in                                                  
      348 +        // this codebase) so a flat union of the closure's par
          +ams and every                                                 
      349 +        // name it `let`-binds anywhere in its body is a consi
          +stent notion of                                               
      350 +        // "bound within the closure" for deciding what must b
          +e captured.                                                   
      351 +        let mut own: HashSet<String> = params.iter().cloned().
          +collect();                                                    
      352 +        collect_let_bound_names(body, &mut own);              
      353 +                                                              
      354 +        let mut referenced: Vec<String> = Vec::new();         
      355 +        let mut seen: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();       
      356 +        collect_referenced_idents(body, &mut referenced, &mut 
          +seen);                                                        
      357 +                                                              
      358 +        // Capture only names that are (a) free in the closure
          + and (b) actually                                             
      359 +        // present in the enclosing scope right now (anything 
          +else is presumably                                            
      360 +        // a host/global function name, not a variable to snap
          +shot).                                                        
      361 +        let captured_names: Vec<String> = referenced.into_iter
          +()                                                            
      362 +            .filter(|n| !own.contains(n) && self.known_locals.
          +contains(n))                                                  
      363 +            .collect();                                       
      364 +                                                              
      365 +        self.closure_counter += 1;                            
      366 +        let func_name = format!("__closure_{}", self.closure_c
          +ounter);                                                      
      367 +        let mut cf = Function {                               
      368 +            name: func_name.clone(),                          
      369 +            params: captured_names.iter().cloned().chain(param
          +s.iter().cloned()).collect(),                                 
      370 +            ..Default::default()                              
      371 +        };                                                    
      372 +        let saved_locals = std::mem::take(&mut self.known_loca
          +ls);                                                          
      373 +        let saved_fname = std::mem::replace(&mut self.current_
          +function, func_name.clone());                                 
      374 +        self.known_locals = cf.params.iter().cloned().collect(
          +);                                                            
      375 +        for st in body { self.lower_stmt(st, &mut cf); }      
      376 +        cf.body.push(Instr::Return);                          
      377 +        self.known_locals = saved_locals;                     
      378 +        self.current_function = saved_fname;                  
      379 +        self.pending_closures.push(cf);                       
      380 +                                                              
      381 +        // At the creation site, push captured values in the s
          +ame order used                                                
      382 +        // for the synthesized function's leading params, then
          + bundle them.                                                 
      383 +        for name in &captured_names {                         
      384 +            f.body.push(Instr::LoadLocal(name.clone()));      
      385 +        }                                                     
      386 +        f.body.push(Instr::MakeClosure(func_name, captured_nam
          +es));                                                         
      387 +    }                                                         
      388  }

      390 +fn collect_let_bound_names(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut HashSet<S
          +tring>) {                                                     
      391 +    for s in stmts {                                          
      392 +        match s {                                             
      393 +            Stmt::Let { name, .. } => { out.insert(name.clone(
          +)); }                                                         
      394 +            Stmt::If { then_branch, else_branch, .. } => {    
      395 +                collect_let_bound_names(then_branch, out);    
      396 +                if let Some(eb) = else_branch { collect_let_bo
          +und_names(eb, out); }                                         
      397 +            }                                                 
      398 +            Stmt::While { body, .. } => collect_let_bound_name
          +s(body, out),                                                 
      399 +            Stmt::When { body, .. } => collect_let_bound_names
          +(body, out),                                                  
      400 +            _ => {}                                           
      401 +        }                                                     
      402 +    }                                                         
      403 +}                                                             
      404 +                                                              
      405 +fn collect_referenced_idents(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<Str
          +ing>, seen: &mut HashSet<String>) {                           
      406 +    for s in stmts {                                          
      407 +        match s {                                             
      408 +            Stmt::ExprStmt(e) => collect_ident_expr(e, out, se
          +en),                                                          
      409 +            Stmt::Let { value, .. } => collect_ident_expr(valu
          +e, out, seen),                                                
      410 +            Stmt::MemberAssign { object, value, .. } => { coll
          +ect_ident_expr(object, out, seen); collect_ident_expr(value, o
          +ut, seen); }                                                  
      411 +            Stmt::Function { body, .. } => collect_referenced_
          +idents(body, out, seen),                                      
      412 +            Stmt::Return(opt) => { if let Some(e) = opt { coll
          +ect_ident_expr(e, out, seen); } }                             
      413 +            Stmt::If { cond, then_branch, else_branch } => {  
      414 +                collect_ident_expr(cond, out, seen);          
      415 +                collect_referenced_idents(then_branch, out, se
          +en);                                                          
      416 +                if let Some(eb) = else_branch { collect_refere
          +nced_idents(eb, out, seen); }                                 
      417 +            }                                                 
      418 +            Stmt::While { cond, body } => { collect_ident_expr
          +(cond, out, seen); collect_referenced_idents(body, out, seen);
          + }                                                            
      419 +            Stmt::Fact { args, .. } | Stmt::Query { args, .. }
          + => { for a in args { collect_ident_expr(a, out, seen); } }   
      420 +            Stmt::When { body, .. } => collect_referenced_iden
          +ts(body, out, seen),                                          
      421 +            Stmt::Assert { expr, .. } => collect_ident_expr(ex
          +pr, out, seen),                                               
      422 +        }                                                     
      423 +    }                                                         
      424 +}                                                             
      425 +                                                              
      426 +fn collect_ident_expr(e: &Expr, out: &mut Vec<String>, seen: &
          +mut HashSet<String>) {                                        
      427 +    match e {                                                 
      428 +        Expr::Identifier(name) => {                           
      429 +            if name != "true" && name != "false" && seen.inser
          +t(name.clone()) {                                             
      430 +                out.push(name.clone());                       
      431 +            }                                                 
      432 +        }                                                     
      433 +        Expr::List(items) => for it in items { collect_ident_e
          +xpr(it, out, seen); },                                        
      434 +        Expr::Member { object, .. } => collect_ident_expr(obje
          +ct, out, seen),                                               
      435 +        Expr::Index { object, index } => { collect_ident_expr(
          +object, out, seen); collect_ident_expr(index, out, seen); }   
      436 +        Expr::UnaryOp { expr, .. } => collect_ident_expr(expr,
          + out, seen),                                                  
      437 +        Expr::BinaryOp { left, right, .. } => { collect_ident_
          +expr(left, out, seen); collect_ident_expr(right, out, seen); }
      438 +        Expr::Call { function, args } => {                    
      439 +            collect_ident_expr(function, out, seen);          
      440 +            for a in args { collect_ident_expr(a, out, seen); 
          +}                                                             
      441 +        }                                                     
      442 +        Expr::Closure { params, body } => {                   
      443 +            // A nested closure's free variables (excluding it
          +s own params) are                                             
      444 +            // also free variables of the enclosing closure, s
          +o they propagate                                              
      445 +            // outward and get captured at whichever scope act
          +ually has them.                                               
      446 +            let mut inner_out = Vec::new();                   
      447 +            let mut inner_seen: HashSet<String> = params.iter(
          +).cloned().collect();                                         
      448 +            collect_referenced_idents(body, &mut inner_out, &m
          +ut inner_seen);                                               
      449 +            for n in inner_out {                              
      450 +                if seen.insert(n.clone()) { out.push(n); }    
      451 +            }                                                 
      452 +        }                                                     
      453 +        Expr::Number(_) | Expr::String(_) => {}               
      454 +    }                                                         
      455 +}                                                             
      456 +

           tract-violation

           ebug dump).
      459  pub fn expr_to_text(e: &Expr) -> String {

      189  
      190 +`apply` calls a function *by name*; real closures — anonymous 
          +functions that                                                
      191 +capture surrounding variables — also exist:                   
      192 +                                                              
      193 +```patlang                                                    
      194 +make a function called make_adder takes n returns r           
      195 +  return |x| { return x + n }     # captures n from the enclos
          +ing call                                                      
      196 +end                                                           
      197 +                                                              
      198 +let add_three = make_adder(3)                                 
      199 +print(add_three(7))               # 10                        
      200 +                                                              
      201 +let inc = |x| { return x + 1 }                                
      202 +make a function called twice takes f, x returns r             
      203 +  return f(f(x))                  # calling a closure held in 
          +a variable                                                    
      204 +end                                                           
      205 +print(twice(inc, 10))             # 12                        
      206 +```                                                           
      207 +                                                              
      208 +Captured variables are snapshotted **by value** at the moment 
          +the closure is                                                
      209 +created — reassigning the outer variable afterwards does not c
          +hange what an                                                 
      210 +already-created closure sees, consistent with this language's 
          +value                                                         
      211 +semantics everywhere else (`list_push` returning a new list ra
          +ther than                                                     
      212 +mutating in place, etc). Closures can be returned from functio
          +ns, stored in                                                 
      213 +variables, passed as arguments, and nested (a closure can itse
          +lf return a                                                   
      214 +closure that captures the outer one's variables).             
      215 +                                                              
      216 +The self-hosted (Stage 1) dialect uses `|params| do ... end` i
          +nstead of                                                     
      217 +Stage 0's `|params| { ... }`, matching the rest of Stage 1's k
          +eyword-based                                                  
      218 +block syntax (which has no brace-delimited blocks anywhere); i
          +t also only                                                   
      219 +supports calling a closure through a named variable, not invok
          +ing a closure                                                 
      220 +literal immediately in place (`(|x| { ... })(5)` — that works

       5 +## status update (July 4, 2026): closures                      
       6 +                                                               
       7 +Real closures landed in both Stage 0 (Rust) and Stage 1 (self-h
         +osted):                                                        
       8 +                                                               
       9 +- `Value::Closure { func_name, captured }` — a closure value is
         + a synthesized                                                 
      10 +  function name plus a snapshot of its captured environment. `I
         +nstr::MakeClosure`                                             
      11 +  bundles N stack values (pushed via `LoadLocal`) into a closur
         +e; `Instr::CallValue`                                          
      12 +  pops a closure value plus call args and dispatches to the syn
         +thesized function                                              
      13 +  (captured values become its leading hidden parameters).      
      14 +- Stage 0 (`rust-runtime/src/ir/lowering.rs`): a real free-vari
         +able analysis                                                  
      15 +  (`collect_referenced_idents`/`collect_let_bound_names`) walks
         + a closure                                                     
      16 +  literal's body, intersects referenced identifiers with the en
         +closing                                                        
      17 +  `known_locals`, and captures exactly those — including transi
         +tively through                                                 
      18 +  nested closures. `Expr::Call` on an identifier now disambigua
         +tes three ways:                                                
      19 +  known top-level function → `Call`, known local variable → `Ca
         +llValue`                                                       
      20 +  (dynamic dispatch through whatever closure value it holds), o
         +therwise → `CallHost`.                                         
      21 +- Stage 1 (`self_hosting/lib/*.patlang`): same design, simplifi
         +ed — rather than                                               
      22 +  precise free-variable analysis, a closure captures its *entir
         +e* enclosing                                                   
      23 +  locals list (over-capture). This is correct by construction g
         +iven the flat                                                  
      24 +  per-call locals map already used throughout: a closure's own 
         +`let` of a                                                     
      25 +  same-named variable just overwrites the pre-bound captured sl
         +ot, which is                                                   
      26 +  exactly the intended shadowing behaviour, so no exclusion log
         +ic is needed.                                                  
      27 +  `locals` and a `pending` (synthesized closure functions) vec 
         +are threaded                                                   
      28 +  as extra parameters through `lower_expr`/`lower_stmt`/`lower_
         +block`.                                                        
      29 +  Closure syntax is `|params| do body end` (not Stage 0's `{ }`
         +), matching                                                    
      30 +  Stage 1's brace-free block convention everywhere else; immedi
         +ately-invoked                                                  
      31 +  closure literals aren't supported yet (the `Call` AST node as
         +sumes a                                                        
      32 +  string callee name — calling through a named variable holding
         + a closure                                                     
      33 +  works fine).

         +ia both the                                                    
      35 +  Stage 0 and Stage 1 codegen paths — nested closures capturing
         + an                                                            
      36 +  enclosing function's own parameter, closures as higher-order 
         +arguments,

         +variable                                                       
      38 +  after closure creation doesn't change what the closure sees).

      281 +                                                              
      282 +event_loop_listen(loop, tcp_listen(0), |conn| do

      295 +

● Update(PATLANG_SELF_HOSTING_ROADMAP.md)
  ⎿  Added 35 lines
       2  
       3  This document outlines the pragmatic next steps to reach self-h
          osting by leveraging the existing PatLang implementations of th
          e lexer, parser, and evaluator, while keeping Stage 0 semantics
           small and focused.
       4  
       5 +## status update (July 4, 2026): async event loop library with 
         +closure callbacks                                              
       6 +                                                               
       7 +`self_hosting/lib/event_loop.patlang`: a reusable JS/Node-style
         + event loop                                                    
       8 +built on the existing `tcp_accept_timeout`/`sleep_ms` primitive
         +s, but with                                                    
       9 +callbacks as ordinary closures (`event_loop_on_tick(loop, cb)`,
      10 +`event_loop_listen(loop, port, cb)`, `event_loop_stop(loop)`,  
      11 +`event_loop_run(loop, poll_ms)`) instead of the `when`/`emit` +
         + `set_var`/`get`                                               
      12 +global-dispatch pattern from `examples/event_loop_server.patlan
         +g` (kept as                                                    
      13 +the earlier, still-working approach for comparison).           
      14 +                                                               
      15 +Key design point: closures snapshot captured variables by value
         +, so any                                                       
      16 +counter that must persist across repeated callback invocations 
         +(e.g. a                                                        
      17 +served-request count) has to live in a mutable cell — here, the
         + object                                                        
      18 +store — rather than a captured plain variable; the closure capt
         +ures the                                                       
      19 +object's *name* (a plain string, cheap to snapshot) and mutates
         + through                                                       
      20 +`get`/`send`, which is visible on every subsequent call. This i
         +s the same                                                     
      21 +value-semantics model used everywhere else in the language (`li
         +st_push`                                                       
      22 +returns a new list; captured closure variables are snapshots), 
         +just applied                                                   
      23 +to callback state.                                             
      24 +                                                               
      25 +Callbacks are invoked via `let cb = get(loop, "on_tick"); cb()`
         + rather than                                                   
      26 +`get(loop, "on_tick")()` directly — the Stage 0 Rust parser sup
         +ports calling                                                  
      27 +the result of an arbitrary expression, but the Stage 1 self-hos
         +ted parser                                                     
      28 +currently only supports calling a named local variable (same li
         +mitation as                                                    
      29 +immediately-invoked closure literals), so routing through a `le
         +t` binding                                                     
      30 +keeps the library source portable across both parsers.         
      31 +                                                               
      32 +`examples/event_loop_demo.patlang`: an HTTP server using the li
         +brary —                                                        
      33 +`on_tick` mutates a shared tick counter, `on_request` serves a 
         +request,                                                       
      34 +updates a shared counter, and calls `event_loop_stop(loop)` fro
         +m inside                                                       
      35 +itself once two requests are served. Compiled through the full 
         +self-hosted                                                    
      36 +pipeline (lexer → parser → lower → codegen → rustc) and verifie
         +d against two                                                  
      37 +real HTTP requests over an actual socket. Regression test:     
      38 +`selfhost_targets.rs::event_loop_closures_serve_and_stop`.     
      39 +                                                               
      40  ## status update (July 4, 2026): closures

          osted):

      271 +pub fn host_file_exists(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Strin
          +g> {                                                          
      272 +    // file_exists(path) -> "1" or "0" (legacy string-bool con
          +vention, kept                                                 
      273 +    // consistent with the codegen template's arm of the same 
          +name)                                                         
      274 +    let p = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
          +one(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() };  
      275 +    let exists = std::path::Path::new(&p).exists();           
      276 +    Ok(Value::String(if exists { "1".into() } else { "0".into(
          +) }))                                                         
      277 +}                                                             
      278 +                                                              
      279 +pub fn host_hash_string(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, Strin
          +g> {                                                          
      280 +    // hash_string(s) -> lowercase hex FNV-1a 64-bit digest. D
          +eterministic                                                  
      281 +    // and stable across runs/platforms; used for build-cache 
          +invalidation                                                  
      282 +    // (compare a source's hash against a previously recorded 
          +one to skip                                                   
      283 +    // an unchanged rustc invocation).                        
      284 +    let s = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
          +one(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() };  
      285 +    let mut hash: u64 = 0xcbf29ce484222325;                   
      286 +    for b in s.as_bytes() {                                   
      287 +        hash ^= *b as u64;                                    
      288 +        hash = hash.wrapping_mul(0x100000001b3);              
      289 +    }                                                         
      290 +    Ok(Value::String(format!("{:016x}", hash)))               
      291 +}                                                             
      292 +                                                              
      293  pub fn host_write_file(args: &[Value]) -> Result<Value, String
           > {
      294      // write_file(path, contents) -> Bool
      295      let p = match args.get(0) { Some(Value::String(s)) => s.cl
           one(), Some(v) => display_value(v), None => String::new() };

  Wrote 1 memory (ctrl+o to expand)

● All four priorities are now complete and committed, with the full test suite
  green throughout (52 suites).

  Summary of this session's work

  Closures (b940f02, split across Stage 0 and Stage 1) — the big one. Real
  closures with genuine free-variable capture: a Value::Closure carrying a
  synthesized function name plus a captured-environment snapshot, dispatched
  through two new instructions (MakeClosure/CallValue) implemented identically
  in the interpreter, the compiled-program template, and the no-rustc run_ir
  path. Stage 0 does precise free-variable analysis; Stage 1 uses a simpler but
  correct-by-construction over-capture strategy (grabs the whole enclosing
  locals list, since the flat per-call locals model means a closure's own let
  just overwrites the pre-bound slot — exactly the shadowing behavior you want).
  Verified end-to-end: nested closures capturing an outer function's own
  parameter, closures as higher-order arguments, value-semantics snapshot
  behavior, and native-compiled parity — all with dedicated regression tests.

  Async event loop (4b4638b) — lib/event_loop.patlang, a JS-style loop where
  callbacks are ordinary closures instead of the when/emit string-dispatch
  pattern. It surfaced a genuinely interesting design point: callback state that
  must persist across calls (a request counter) can't live in a captured
  variable — closures snapshot by value — so it lives in the object store
  instead, with the closure capturing the object's name. Demonstrated with a
  real HTTP server that serves two requests and stops itself from inside its own
  callback.

  Build time/size (676342f) — WASM builds now auto-detect and use -C opt-level=z
  -C strip=symbols, cutting the portfolio's playground.wasm from 2.7MB to 593KB
  (and compiling faster), with output verified byte-identical. More importantly
  for your actual pain point this session: portfolio build caching, keyed on a
  hash of both each demo's source and a fingerprint of the whole compiler
  pipeline (so a compiler change safely invalidates everything, but editing one
  demo only rebuilds that one) — measured 14m05s → 31s, a 27× speedup, with
  every cached artifact verified byte-reused and still functionally correct.

  One thing worth flagging honestly: the WASM size optimization trades away some
  runtime speed (the in-browser fib(27) benchmark now reports ~300ms instead of
  ~160ms) — documented plainly in the tutorial rather than hidden, since it's a
  real tradeoff appropriate for a demo/portability use case but not universally
  free.
